node_management/optimization/resource_allocation.py

class ResourceAllocator:
    def __init__(self, node_manager: NodeLifeCycleManager):
        self.node_manager = node_manager

    def allocate_resources(self, task: dict) -> str:
        """Allocate resources for a given task based on node availability and capacity."""
        eligible_nodes = self._find_eligible_nodes(task)
        if eligible_nodes:
            chosen_node = self._choose_node(eligible_nodes)
            chosen_node.assign_task(task)
            return chosen_node.node_id
        else:
            print(f"No eligible node found for task: {task['task_id']}")
            return None

    def _find_eligible_nodes(self,

---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
Python

 task: dict) -> list:
        """Find nodes that are eligible to execute a given task."""
        eligible_nodes = []
        for node in self.node_manager.nodes.values():
            if node.status == "idle" and node.role == task["required_role"]:
                # Check if node has enough capacity
                if self._calculate_available_capacity(node) >= task.get("required_capacity", 0):
                    eligible_nodes.append(node)
        return eligible_nodes

    def _calculate_available_capacity(self, node: Node) -> float:
        """Calculate the available capacity of a node."""
        used_capacity = len(node.tasks)  # Simplistic: each task uses 1 unit of capacity
        return node.capacity - used_capacity

    def _choose_node(self, eligible_nodes: list) -> Node:
        """Choose a node from the list of eligible nodes based on a specific strategy."""
        # Strategy: Choose the node with the most available capacity
        return max(eligible_nodes, key=lambda node: self._calculate_available_capacity(node))

    def reallocate_resources(self):
        """Reallocate resources across all nodes to optimize performance and energy usage."""
        # Example: Move tasks from overloaded nodes to underloaded nodes
        overloaded_nodes = [node for node in self.node_manager.nodes.values() if self._is_overloaded(node)]
        underloaded_nodes = [node for node in self.node_manager.nodes.values() if self._is_underloaded(node)]

        for overloaded_node in overloaded_nodes:
            while self._is_overloaded(overloaded_node) and underloaded_nodes:
                task_to_move = overloaded_node.tasks.pop()  # Remove task from overloaded node
                target_node = underloaded_nodes.pop(0)
                target_node.assign_task(task_to_move)
                print(f"Task {task_to_move['task_id']} moved from {overloaded_node.node_id} to {target_node.node_id}")
                if not self._is_underloaded(target_node):
                    underloaded_nodes.insert(0, target_node)  # Put it back if it's no longer underloaded

    def _is_overloaded(self, node: Node, threshold: float = 0.8) -> bool:
        """Check if a node is overloaded."""
        return self._calculate_available_capacity(node) < (1 - threshold) * node.capacity

    def _is_underloaded(self, node: Node, threshold: float = 0.5) -> bool:
        """Check if a node is underloaded."""
        return self._calculate_available_capacity(node) > threshold * node.capacity
    
    def get_state(self) -> dict:
      """Return the current state of the resource allocator."""
      return {
          'node_loads': {node_id: {'capacity': node.capacity, 'available_capacity': self._calculate_available_capacity(node)} for node_id, node in self.node_manager.nodes.items()}
      }

node_management/optimization/task_prioritization.py
Python

class TaskPrioritizer:
    def __init__(self, node_manager: NodeLifeCycleManager):
        self.node_manager = node_manager

    def prioritize_tasks(self, tasks: list) -> list:
        """Prioritize tasks based on their priority and required resources."""
        # Sort tasks by priority (higher value means higher priority) and then by required capacity
        return sorted(tasks, key=lambda task: (task.get('priority', 0), -task.get('required_capacity', 0)), reverse=True)

    def update_task_priority(self, task_id: str, new_priority: int):
        """Update the priority of a specific task."""
        for node in self.node_manager.nodes.values():
            for task in node.tasks:
                if task['task_id'] == task_id:
                    task['priority'] = new_priority
                    return

    def get_prioritized_tasks(self, node_id: str = None) -> list:
        """Get a list of tasks prioritized, optionally for a specific node."""
        if node_id:
            node = self.node_manager.get_node(node_id)
            if node:
                return self.prioritize_tasks(node.tasks)
            else:
                return []
        else:
            all_tasks = []
            for node in self.node_manager.nodes.values():
                all_tasks.extend(node.tasks)
            return self.prioritize_tasks(all_tasks)
        
    def get_state(self) -> dict:
      """Return the current state of the task prioritizer."""
      return {
          'prioritized_tasks': self.get_prioritized_tasks()
      }

system_monitoring/metrics/performance_tracker.py
Python

import psutil
import time

class PerformanceTracker:
    def __init__(self, node_manager: NodeLifeCycleManager):
        self.node_manager = node_manager
        self.performance_data = {}
        self.last_update = time.time()

    def collect_performance_data(self):
        """Collect performance metrics for each node."""
        current_time = time.time()
        for node_id, node in self.node_manager.nodes.items():
            try:
                # CPU usage
                cpu_usage = psutil.cpu_percent(interval=1, percpu=True)

                # RAM usage
                ram = psutil.virtual_memory()
                ram_usage = ram.percent
                ram_total = ram.total
                ram_available = ram.available

                # Disk usage
                disk = psutil.disk_usage('/')  # Assuming root partition
                disk_usage = disk.percent
                disk_total = disk.total
                disk_free = disk.free

                # Network usage
                net_io_counters = psutil.net_io_counters()
                bytes_sent = net_io_counters.bytes_sent
                bytes_recv = net_io_counters.bytes_recv

                # Update performance data
                self.performance_data[node_id] = {
                    'timestamp': current_time,
                    'cpu_usage': cpu_usage,
                    'ram_usage': ram_usage,
                    'ram_total': ram_total,
                    'ram_available': ram_available,
                    'disk_usage': disk_usage,
                    'disk_total': disk_total,
                    'disk_free': disk_free,
                    'bytes_sent': bytes_sent,
                    'bytes_recv': bytes_recv
                }

            except Exception as e:
                print(f"Error collecting performance data for node {node_id}: {e}")

        self.last_update = current_time

    def get_performance_data(self, node_id: str = None) -> dict:
        """Get the latest performance data, optionally for a specific node."""
        if node_id:
            return self.performance_data.get(node_id, {})
        else:
            return self.performance_data

    def get_historical_performance_data(self, node_id: str, time_range: int = 3600) -> list:
        """Get historical performance data for a node within a specific time range."""
        historical_data = []
        current_time = time.time()
        for data_point in reversed(self.performance_data.get(node_id, [])):
            if current_time - data_point['timestamp'] <= time_range:
                historical_data.append(data_point)
            else:
                break
        return historical_data
    
    def get_state(self) -> dict:
      """Return the current state of the performance tracker."""
      return {
          'last_update': self.last_update,
          'performance_data': self.performance_data
      }

system_monitoring/metrics/health_monitor.py

import time

class HealthMonitor:
    def __init__(self, node_manager: NodeLifeCycleManager):
        self.node_manager = node_manager
        self.health_status = {}
        self.last_check = time.time()

    def check_health(self):
        """Check the health status of each node."""
        current_time = time.time()
        for node_id, node in self.node_manager.nodes.items():
            # Check heartbeat
            if current_time - node.last_heartbeat > 30:  # Example threshold
                status = "unhealthy"
            else:
                status = "healthy"

            # Check task status
            for task in node.tasks:
                if task.get("status") == "failed":
                    status = "unhealthy"
                    break

            self.health_status[node_id] = {
                'timestamp': current_time,
                'status': status
            }

        self.last_check = current_time

    def get_health_status(self, node_id: str = None) -> dict:
        """Get the latest health status, optionally for a specific node."""
        if node_id:
            return self.health_status.get(node_id, {})
        else:
            return self.health_status

    def get_unhealthy_nodes(self) -> list:
        """Get a list of unhealthy nodes."""
        return [node_id for node_id, status in self.health_status.items() if status['status'] == "unhealthy"]

    def get_state(self) -> dict:
        """Returns the current state of the health monitor."""
        return {
            'last_check': self.last_check,
            'health_status': self.health_status
        }

system_monitoring/visualization/metrics_display.py

import matplotlib.pyplot as plt
import matplotlib.animation as animation
import time
from collections import deque

class MetricsDisplay:
    def __init__(self, performance_tracker: PerformanceTracker, update_interval: int = 5000):
        self.performance_tracker = performance_tracker
        self.update_interval = update_interval  # in milliseconds
        self.fig, self.axs = plt.subplots(2, 2, figsize=(12, 8))  # Adjust for desired metrics
        self.data_buffers = {}

        # Initialize data buffers for each metric
        for node_id in self.performance_tracker.node_manager.nodes.keys():
            self.data_buffers[node_id] = {
                'timestamps': deque(maxlen=60),  # Store data for the last 60 intervals
                'cpu_usage': deque(maxlen=60),
                'ram_usage': deque(maxlen=60),
                'disk_usage': deque(maxlen=60),
                'network_sent': deque(maxlen=60),
                'network_recv': deque(maxlen=60)
            }

        self.setup_plots()

    def setup_plots(self):
      """Set up the initial plots."""
      metrics = ['cpu_usage', 'ram_usage', 'disk_usage', 'network_sent']
      colors = ['r', 'g', 'b', 'y']  # Colors for different metrics
  
      for ax, metric, color in zip(self.axs.flat, metrics, colors):
          ax.set_title(metric.upper())
          ax.set_xlabel('Time (s)')
          ax.set_ylabel('%' if metric != 'network_sent' else 'Bytes')
          ax.set_ylim(0, 100 if metric != 'network_sent' else 0, 50000)  # Initial y-axis limit for network_sent
  
          # Create a line for each node
          for node_id in self.performance_tracker.node_manager.nodes.keys():
              line, = ax.plot([], [], '-', label=f'{node_id}', color=color, alpha=0.7) # added color and transparency for each node
              
              # Store the line objects for updating later
              if 'lines' not in self.data_buffers[node_id]:
                  self.data_buffers[node_id]['lines'] = {}
              self.data_buffers[node_id]['lines'][metric] = line
  
          ax.legend()
          ax.grid(True)
  
      plt.tight_layout()
  
    def update_data(self):
        """Update data buffers with the latest performance data."""
        self.performance_tracker.collect_performance_data()
        current_time = time.time()

        for node_id, performance_data in self.performance_tracker.performance_data.items():
            if node_id in self.data_buffers:
                self.data_buffers[node_id]['timestamps'].append(current_time)
                self.data_buffers[node_id]['cpu_usage'].append(np.mean(performance_data['cpu_usage']))
                self.data_buffers[node_id]['ram_usage'].append(performance_data['ram_usage'])
                self.data_buffers[node_id]['disk_usage'].append(performance_data['disk_usage'])
                
                # Calculate network sent/received rate
                if len(self.data_buffers[node_id]['network_sent']) > 0:
                    time_diff = current_time - self.data_buffers[node_id]['timestamps'][-2]
                    sent_diff = performance_data['bytes_sent'] - self.data_buffers[node_id]['network_sent'][-1]
                    recv_diff = performance_data['bytes_recv'] - self.data_buffers[node_id]['network_recv'][-1]
                    self.data_buffers[node_id]['network_sent'].append(sent_diff / time_diff if time_diff > 0 else 0)
                    self.data_buffers[node_id]['network_recv'].append(recv_diff / time_diff if time_diff > 0 else 0)
                else:
                    self.data_buffers[node_id]['network_sent'].append(0)
                    self.data_buffers[node_id]['network_recv'].append(0)

    def update_plots(self, i):
        """Update the plots with the latest data."""
        self.update_data()

        for ax, metric in zip(self.axs.flat, ['cpu_usage', 'ram_usage', 'disk_usage', 'network_sent']):
            for node_id in self.performance_tracker.node_manager.nodes.keys():
                if node_id in self.data_buffers and metric in self.data_buffers[node_id]:
                    x_data = list(self.data_buffers[node_id]['timestamps'])
                    y_data = list(self.data_buffers[node_id][metric])

                    # Update line data
                    self.data_buffers[node_id]['lines'][metric].set_data(x_data, y_data)

                    # Rescale the axes if needed
                    if y_data:
                        ax.set_xlim(min(x_data), max(x_data))
                        if metric != 'network_sent':
                            ax.set_ylim(0, max(100, max(y_data) * 1.1))
                        else:
                            ax.set_ylim(0, max(y_data) * 1.1)  # Adjust for network_sent

        return [line for node_data in self.data_buffers.values() for line in node_data['lines'].values()]

    def animate(self):
      """Start the animation."""
      ani = animation.FuncAnimation(
          self.fig,
          self.update_plots,
          interval=self.update_interval,
          blit=False  # Changed blit to False
      )
      plt.show()

    def get_state(self) -> dict:
        """Returns the current state of the metrics display."""
        return {
            'update_interval': self.update_interval,
            'data_buffers': {node_id: {metric: list(data) for metric, data in node_data.items() if metric != 'lines'} for node_id, node_data in self.data_buffers.items()}
        }

import networkx as nx
import matplotlib.pyplot as plt
import time

class SystemVisualizer:
    def __init__(self, node_manager: NodeLifeCycleManager):
        self.node_manager = node_manager
        self.graph = nx.Graph()
        self.positions = {}  # Store positions for nodes
        self.last_update = time.time()

    def update_graph(self):
        """Update the graph based on the current system state."""
        self.graph.clear()
        self.positions.clear()

        # Add nodes to the graph
        for node_id, node in self.node_manager.nodes.items():
            self.graph.add_node(node_id, status=node.status, role=node.role)

        # Add failed nodes to the graph
        for node_id, node in self.node_manager.failed_nodes.items():
            self.graph.add_node(node_


----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
Python

_id, status=node.status, role=node.role)

        # Define positions for different roles (for visualization)
        role_positions = {
            "kaleidoscope_core": (0, 0),
            "perspective_engine": (1, 1),
            "data_processing": (1, -1),
            "system_monitoring": (-1, -1),
            "auxiliary": (-1, 1),
            "failed": (0, -2)
        }

        # Calculate positions for nodes
        for node_id, node_data in self.graph.nodes(data=True):
            role = node_data['role']
            if role in role_positions:
                if role not in self.positions:
                    self.positions[role] = list(role_positions[role])
                else:
                    # Increment position for nodes with the same role
                    self.positions[role][0] += 0.2 
                    self.positions[role][1] += 0.2
                self.positions[node_id] = tuple(self.positions[role])
            else:
                # Default position for unknown roles
                self.positions[node_id] = (0, 0)

        self.last_update = time.time()

    def visualize_system(self, save_to_file: bool = False, filename: str = 'system_visualization.png'):
        """Visualize the system state."""
        self.update_graph()

        # Draw the graph
        node_colors = ['lightgreen' if data['status'] == 'idle' else 'red' if data['status'] == 'failed' else 'skyblue' for node, data in self.graph.nodes(data=True)]
        nx.draw(self.graph, self.positions, with_labels=True, node_color=node_colors, node_size=1000, font_size=10)

        # Add labels for roles
        for role, pos in self.positions.items():
            if isinstance(role, str):  # Check if the key is a role name
                plt.text(pos[0], pos[1] - 0.2, role, horizontalalignment='center', fontsize=12, fontweight='bold')

        # Save to file or show the plot
        if save_to_file:
            plt.savefig(filename)
            plt.close()
        else:
            plt.show()
            
    def get_state(self) -> dict:
        """Returns the current state of the system visualizer."""
        return {
            'graph': {
                'nodes': list(self.graph.nodes(data=True)),
                'edges': list(self.graph.edges(data=True))
            },
            'positions': self.positions,
            'last_update': self.last_update
        }

system_monitoring/logging/system_logger.py
Python

import logging
import os
from datetime import datetime

class SystemLogger:
    def __init__(self, log_directory: str = "logs"):
        self.log_directory = log_directory
        if not os.path.exists(log_directory):
            os.makedirs(log_directory)
        self.loggers = {}

    def get_logger(self, name: str, log_level: int = logging.INFO) -> logging.Logger:
        """Get a logger with the specified name."""
        if name in self.loggers:
            return self.loggers[name]

        logger = logging.getLogger(name)
        logger.setLevel(log_level)

        # Create a unique filename for each logger
        timestamp = datetime.now().strftime("%Y%m%d-%H%M%S")
        log_file = os.path.join(self.log_directory, f"{name}-{timestamp}.log")

        # Create a file handler and set the level
        file_handler = logging.FileHandler(log_file)
        file_handler.setLevel(log_level)

        # Create a formatter and add it to the handler
        formatter = logging.Formatter('%(asctime)s - %(name)s - %(levelname)s - %(message)s')
        file_handler.setFormatter(formatter)

        # Add the handler to the logger
        logger.addHandler(file_handler)

        self.loggers[name] = logger
        return logger

    def log_message(self, logger_name: str, message: str, level: str = "info"):
        """Log a message with the specified logger and level."""
        logger = self.get_logger(logger_name)
        if level == "info":
            logger.info(message)
        elif level == "warning":
            logger.warning(message)
        elif level == "error":
            logger.error(message)
        elif level == "critical":
            logger.critical(message)
        else:
            raise ValueError(f"Invalid log level: {level}")
            
    def get_state(self) -> dict:
        """Returns the current state of the system logger."""
        return {
            'log_directory': self.log_directory,
            'active_loggers': list(self.loggers.keys())
        }

system_monitoring/logging/error_tracker.py
Python

import traceback
from datetime import datetime

class ErrorTracker:
    def __init__(self, system_logger: SystemLogger):
        self.system_logger = system_logger
        self.error_log = []

    def track_error(self, error: Exception, context_info: dict = None):
        """Track an error with relevant context information."""
        timestamp = datetime.now()
        error_message = str(error)
        stack_trace = traceback.format_exc()

        error_record = {
            'timestamp': timestamp,
            'error_message': error_message,
            'stack_trace': stack_trace,
            'context': context_info
        }
        self.error_log.append(error_record)

        # Log the error using the SystemLogger
        logger = self.system_logger.get_logger("error_tracker")
        logger.error(f"Error: {error_message}\nContext: {context_info}\nStack Trace:\n{stack_trace}")

    def get_recent_errors(self, n: int = 10) -> list:
        """Get the n most recent error records."""
        return self.error_log[-n:]

    def clear_errors(self):
        """Clear the error log."""
        self.error_log = []
        
    def get_state(self) -> dict:
        """Returns the current state of the error tracker."""
        return {
            'error_count': len(self.error_log),
            'last_error': self.error_log[-1] if self.error_log else None
        }

data_processing/realtime/data_processor.py

import numpy as np

class DataProcessor:
    def __init__(self, kaleidoscope_engine, perspective_engine):
        self.kaleidoscope_engine = kaleidoscope_engine
        self.perspective_engine = perspective_engine

    def process_data(self, data: np.ndarray) -> dict:
        """Process data through the Kaleidoscope and Perspective Engines."""
        # Process data through the Kaleidoscope Engine
        kaleidoscope_output = self.kaleidoscope_engine.process_data(data)

        # Process data through the Perspective Engine
        perspective_output = self.perspective_engine.process_data(data)

        # Combine outputs
        combined_output = {
            'kaleidoscope': kaleidoscope_output,
            'perspective': perspective_output
        }

        return combined_output

    def process_and_analyze(self, data: np.ndarray) -> dict:
      """
      Process data through the Kaleidoscope and Perspective Engines, 
      and perform additional analysis or post-processing.
      """
      # Process data through the Kaleidoscope Engine
      kaleidoscope_output = self.kaleidoscope_engine.process_data(data)
  
      # Process data through the Perspective Engine
      perspective_output = self.perspective_engine.process_data(data)
      
      # Perform additional analysis (example: analyze patterns from Perspective Engine)
      resonance_patterns = perspective_output.get('resonances', [])
      insights = perspective_output.get('insights', [])
      
      advanced_resonance_processor = self.perspective_engine.advanced_resonance_processor
      advanced_resonance_output = advanced_resonance_processor.process_advanced_resonance(data)
      
      # Analyze patterns using AdvancedResonanceProcessor
      # combined_patterns = resonance_patterns + advanced_resonance_output['advanced_patterns']
      
      # temporal_patterns = advanced_resonance_processor.pattern_synthesis['temporal'].process(combined_patterns)
      # spatial_patterns = advanced_resonance_processor.pattern_synthesis['spatial'].process(combined_patterns)
      # causal_patterns = advanced_resonance_processor.pattern_synthesis['causal'].process(combined_patterns)

      # Synthesize higher-order patterns using AdvancedResonanceProcessor
      # advanced_patterns = advanced_resonance_processor._synthesize_advanced_patterns(
      #     temporal_patterns, spatial_patterns, causal_patterns
      # )

      # Combine outputs
      combined_output = {
          'kaleidoscope': kaleidoscope_output,
          'perspective': perspective_output,
          'advanced_resonance': advanced_resonance_output,
          # 'temporal_patterns': temporal_patterns,
          # 'spatial_patterns': spatial_patterns,
          # 'causal_patterns': causal_patterns,
          # 'advanced_patterns': advanced_patterns
      }
  
      return combined_output
    
    def get_state(self) -> dict:
        """Returns the current state of the data processor."""
        return {
            'kaleidoscope_engine_state': self.kaleidoscope_engine.get_state(),
            'perspective_engine_state': self.perspective_engine.get_state()
        }

data_processing/realtime/stream_handler.py
Python

import time
import queue
import threading

class StreamHandler:
    def __init__(self, data_processor: DataProcessor, batch_size: int = 10, processing_interval: float = 1.0):
        self.data_processor = data_processor
        self.batch_size = batch_size
        self.processing_interval = processing_interval
        self.data_queue = queue.Queue()
        self.is_running = False
        self.processing_thread = None

    def add_data(self, data):
        """Add data to the processing queue."""
        self.data_queue.put(data)

    def start_processing(self):
        """Start the data processing thread."""
        if not self.is_running:
            self.is_running = True
            self.processing_thread = threading.Thread(target=self._process_data_loop)
            self.processing_thread.start()

    def stop_processing(self):
        """Stop the data processing thread."""
        if self.is_running:
            self.is_running = False
            if self.processing_thread:
                self.processing_thread.join()

    def _process_data_loop(self):
        """Continuously process data from the queue."""
        while self.is_running:
            batch = []
            while len(batch) < self.batch_size:
                try:
                    data = self.data_queue.get(timeout=self.processing_interval)
                    batch.append(data)
                except queue.Empty:
                    break  # No more data in the queue

            if batch:
                # Process the batch of data
                processed_data = self.data_processor.process_and_analyze(batch)
                # Handle the processed data (e.g., store it, visualize it, etc.)
                print("Processed Data:", processed_data)

            time.sleep(self.processing_interval)  # Wait for the next processing interval
            
    def get_state(self) -> dict:
        """Returns the current state of the stream handler."""
        return {
            'batch_size': self.batch_size,
            'processing_interval': self.processing_interval,
            'data_queue_size': self.data_queue.qsize(),
            'is_running': self.is_running
        }

data_processing/historical/data_analyzer.py

import numpy as np

class DataAnalyzer:
    def __init__(self, data_storage):
        self.data_storage = data_storage

    def analyze_historical_data(self, start_time, end_time) -> dict:
        """Analyze historical data within a specific time range."""
        data = self.data_storage.retrieve_data(start_time, end_time)
        if not data:
            return {}

        # Convert data to NumPy array for analysis
        data_array = np.array(data)

        # Perform analysis
        analysis = {
            'start_time': start_time,
            'end_time': end_time,
            'data_points': len(data_array),
            'mean': np.mean(data_array, axis=0).tolist(),
            'std': np.std(data_array, axis=0).tolist(),
            'min': np.min(data_array, axis=0).tolist(),
            'max': np.max(data_array, axis=0).tolist(),
            'correlations': self._calculate_correlations(data_array)
        }

        return analysis

    def _calculate_correlations(self, data_array: np.ndarray) -> dict:
        """Calculate correlations between different data dimensions."""
        num_dimensions = data_array.shape[1]
        correlations = {}

        for i in range(num_dimensions):
            for j in range(i + 1, num_dimensions):
                correlation = np.corrcoef(data_array[:, i], data_array[:, j])[0, 1]
                correlations[f'dim_{i}_vs_dim_{j}'] = correlation

        return correlations

    def get_state(self) -> dict:
        """Returns the current state of the data analyzer."""
        return {
            'data_storage_size': len(self.data_storage.data),
            'last_analysis': self.analyze_historical_data(self.data_storage.data[0]['timestamp'], self.data_storage.data[-1]['timestamp']) if self.data_storage.data else None
        }

import numpy as np

class PatternExtractor:
    def __init__(self, data_analyzer: DataAnalyzer):
        self.data_analyzer = data_analyzer
        self.extracted_patterns = []

    def extract_patterns(self, start_time, end_time, pattern_type: str) -> list:
        """Extract patterns of a specific type from historical data."""
        analysis_results = self.data_analyzer.analyze_historical_data(start_time, end_time)
        if not analysis_results:
            return []

        patterns = []
        if pattern_type == 'trend':
            patterns = self._extract_trends(analysis_results)
        elif pattern_type == 'anomaly':
            patterns = self._extract_anomalies(analysis_results)
        # Add more pattern types as needed

        self.extracted_patterns.extend(patterns)
        return patterns

    def _extract_trends(self, analysis_results: dict) -> list:
        """Extract trend patterns from analysis results."""
        trends = []
        for i in range(len(analysis_results['mean'])):
            # Example: Detect increasing or decreasing trends based on mean values
            if analysis_results['mean'][i] > analysis_results['mean'][0] * 1.1:  # 10% increase
                trends.append({
                    'type': 'trend',
                    'pattern': 'increasing',
                    'dimension': i,
                    'start_time': analysis_results['start_time'],
                    'end_time': analysis_results['end_time']
                })
            elif analysis_results['mean'][i] < analysis_results['mean'][0] * 0.9:  # 10% decrease
                trends.append({
                    'type': 'trend',
                    'pattern': 'decreasing',
                    'dimension': i,
                    'start_time': analysis_results['start_time'],
                    'end_time': analysis_results['end_time']
                })

        return trends

    def _extract_anomalies(self, analysis_results: dict) -> list:
      """
      Extract anomaly patterns from analysis results.
      Anomalies are data points that deviate significantly from the mean, considering the standard deviation.
      """
      anomalies = []
      data = self.data_analyzer.data_storage.retrieve_data(analysis_results['start_time'], analysis_results['end_time'])
      data_array = np.array(data)

      for i in range(data_array.shape[1]):  # Iterate over each dimension
          for j, value in enumerate(data_array[:, i]):
              # Calculate the z-score to determine how many standard deviations a data point is from the mean
              z_score = (value - analysis_results['mean'][i]) / analysis_results['std'][i] if analysis_results['std'][i] > 0



--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
Python

              # Consider a data point an anomaly if its z-score is above a certain threshold (e.g., 2.5)
              if abs(z_score) > 2.5:
                  anomalies.append({
                      'type': 'anomaly',
                      'dimension': i,
                      'value': value,
                      'z_score': z_score,
                      'timestamp': data[j]['timestamp'],
                      'data_index': j
                  })

      return anomalies

    def get_state(self) -> dict:
        """Returns the current state of the pattern extractor."""
        return {
            'extracted_patterns_count': len(self.extracted_patterns),
            'last_extraction': {
                'start_time': self.extracted_patterns[-1]['start_time'] if self.extracted_patterns else None,
                'end_time': self.extracted_patterns[-1]['end_time'] if self.extracted_patterns else None,
                'pattern_type': self.extracted_patterns[-1]['type'] if self.extracted_patterns else None
            }
        }

class DataPipeline:
    def __init__(self, stream_handler: StreamHandler, data_storage, historical_analyzer: DataAnalyzer, pattern_extractor: PatternExtractor):
        self.stream_handler = stream_handler
        self.data_storage = data_storage
        self.historical_analyzer = historical_analyzer
        self.pattern_extractor = pattern_extractor

    def run_pipeline(self):
        """Start the data pipeline, including stream handling and historical analysis."""
        self.stream_handler.start_processing()

        # Example: Trigger historical analysis periodically
        # You might want to replace this with a more sophisticated scheduling mechanism
        import time
        while True:
            time.sleep(3600)  # Analyze historical data every hour
            self.analyze_historical_data()

    def analyze_historical_data(self):
        """Perform historical data analysis and pattern extraction."""
        end_time = time.time()
        start_time = end_time - 86400  # Analyze data from the last 24 hours

        analysis_results = self.historical_analyzer.analyze_historical_data(start_time, end_time)
        print("Historical Analysis Results:", analysis_results)

        extracted_patterns = self.pattern_extractor.extract_patterns(start_time, end_time, 'trend')
        print("Extracted Trend Patterns:", extracted_patterns)

        anomalies = self.pattern_extractor.extract_patterns(start_time, end_time, 'anomaly')
        print("Extracted Anomalies:", anomalies)
        
    def get_state(self) -> dict:
      """Return the current state of the data pipeline."""
      return {
          'stream_handler_state': self.stream_handler.get_state(),
          'data_storage_state': self.data_storage.get_state(),
          'historical_analyzer_state': self.historical_analyzer.get_state(),
          'pattern_extractor_state': self.pattern_extractor.get_state()
      }

data_processing/integration/transformation_engine.py
Python

import numpy as np

class TransformationEngine:
    def __init__(self, dimensions: int):
        self.dimensions = dimensions
        self.transformation_matrices = self._initialize_matrices()

    def _initialize_matrices(self):
        """Initialize transformation matrices for each dimension."""
        matrices = {}
        for dim in range(self.dimensions):
            matrices[dim] = np.eye(dim + 1)  # Example: Identity matrix
        return matrices

    def transform_data(self, data: np.ndarray, dimension: int) -> np.ndarray:
        """Transform data using a specified transformation matrix."""
        if dimension not in self.transformation_matrices:
            raise ValueError(f"Dimension {dimension} not found.")

        matrix = self.transformation_matrices[dimension]

        # Ensure data is in the correct shape for matrix multiplication
        if data.shape[0] != matrix.shape[1]:
            padded_data = np.pad(data, (0, matrix.shape[1] - data.shape[0]), 'constant')
        else:
            padded_data = data

        transformed_data = np.dot(matrix, padded_data)
        return transformed_data

    def update_transformation_matrix(self, dimension: int, matrix: np.ndarray):
        """Update the transformation matrix for a given dimension."""
        if dimension not in self.transformation_matrices:
            raise ValueError(f"Dimension {dimension} not found.")

        if matrix.shape != self.transformation_matrices[dimension].shape:
            raise ValueError("Matrix shape mismatch.")

        self.transformation_matrices[dimension] = matrix

    def get_transformation_matrix(self, dimension: int) -> np.ndarray:
        """Get the transformation matrix for a specific dimension."""
        if dimension not in self.transformation_matrices:
            raise ValueError(f"Dimension {dimension} not found.")

        return self.transformation_matrices[dimension]
    
    def get_state(self) -> dict:
        """Returns the current state of the transformation engine."""
        return {
            'dimensions': self.dimensions,
            'transformation_matrices': {dim: matrix.tolist() for dim, matrix in self.transformation_matrices.items()}
        }

utils/quantum_math.py
Python

import numpy as np
import scipy.linalg as spl

class QuantumMath:

    @staticmethod
    def create_entangled_state(num_dimensions: int) -> np.ndarray:
        """Create a maximally entangled state for a given number of dimensions."""
        state = np.zeros(2 ** num_dimensions, dtype=np.complex128)
        state[0] = 1 / np.sqrt(2)
        state[-1] = 1 / np.sqrt(2)
        return state

    @staticmethod
    def apply_hadamard(state: np.ndarray) -> np.ndarray:
        """Apply the Hadamard transformation to a given quantum state."""
        dim = int(np.log2(len(state)))  # Infer the number of qubits from state size
        hadamard_gate = (1 / np.sqrt(2)) * np.array([[1, 1], [1, -1]])
        
        # Create a tensor product of Hadamard gates for the full system
        full_hadamard = hadamard_gate
        for _ in range(1, dim):
            full_hadamard = np.kron(full_hadamard, hadamard_gate)

        return np.dot(full_hadamard, state)

    @staticmethod
    def measure_state(state: np.ndarray) -> int:
        """Perform a measurement on the given quantum state."""
        probabilities = np.abs(state) ** 2
        return np.random.choice(len(state), p=probabilities)

    @staticmethod
    def calculate_density_matrix(state: np.ndarray) -> np.ndarray:
      """Calculate the density matrix for a given quantum state."""
      return np.outer(state, np.conj(state))

    @staticmethod
    def calculate_purity(state: np.ndarray) -> float:
      """Calculate the purity of a given quantum state."""
      density_matrix = QuantumMath.calculate_density_matrix(state)
      return np.real(np.trace(np.dot(density_matrix, density_matrix)))
    
    @staticmethod
    def calculate_entanglement_entropy(state: np.ndarray) -> float:
      """Calculate the entanglement entropy of a given quantum state."""
      # Assuming the state represents a bipartite system of equal size
      dim = int(len(state) ** 0.5)
      
      # Reshape the state into a matrix
      reshaped_state = state.reshape((dim, dim))

      # Perform singular value decomposition
      _, s, _ = np.linalg.svd(reshaped_state)

      # Calculate probabilities from singular values
      probabilities = s**2 / np.sum(s**2)

      # Calculate entropy
      entropy = -np.sum(probabilities * np.log2(probabilities + 1e-10))
      
      return entropy

    @staticmethod
    def unitary_transform(state: np.ndarray, matrix: np.ndarray) -> np.ndarray:
      """
      Apply a unitary transformation to a quantum state.
      
      Args:
          state: The quantum state vector.
          matrix: The unitary transformation matrix.
          
      Returns:
          The transformed quantum state.
      """
      if not QuantumMath.is_unitary(matrix):
          raise ValueError("The provided matrix is not unitary.")
      
      return np.dot(matrix, state)
    
    @staticmethod
    def is_unitary(matrix: np.ndarray, tolerance: float = 1e-10) -> bool:
      """
      Check if a matrix is unitary.
      
      Args:
          matrix: The matrix to check.
          tolerance: The tolerance for checking if the matrix is unitary.
          
      Returns:
          True if the matrix is unitary, False otherwise.
      """
      
      # Calculate the conjugate transpose
      matrix_dagger = np.conj(matrix).T
      
      # Calculate the product of the matrix and its conjugate transpose
      product = np.dot(matrix, matrix_dagger)
      
      # Check if the product is close to the identity matrix
      identity = np.eye(matrix.shape[0])
      return np.allclose(product, identity, atol=tolerance)

import numpy as np
from collections import Counter

class PatternAnalysis:

    @staticmethod
    def calculate_pattern_frequency(patterns: list) -> dict:
        """Calculate the frequency of each pattern type."""
        pattern_types = [pattern['type'] for pattern in patterns]
        return dict(Counter(pattern_types))

    @staticmethod
    def calculate_average_pattern_strength(patterns: list) -> float:
        """Calculate the average strength of patterns."""
        strengths = [pattern.get('strength', 0) for pattern in patterns]
        return np.mean(strengths) if strengths else 0

    @staticmethod
    def find_most_common_dimension(patterns: list) -> int:
      """Find the most common dimension involved in patterns."""
      dimensions = []
      for pattern in patterns:
          if 'dimensions' in pattern:
              dimensions.extend(pattern['dimensions'])
          elif 'dimension' in pattern:
              dimensions.append(pattern['dimension'])
      
      if not dimensions:
          return None

      # Find the most common dimension
      dimension_counts = Counter(dimensions)
      most_common_dimension = dimension_counts.most_common(1)[0][0]
      return most_common_dimension

    @staticmethod
    def calculate_pattern_correlations(patterns: list) -> dict:
        """Calculate correlations between different pattern attributes."""
        correlations = {}
        
        # Extract attributes from patterns
        attributes = defaultdict(list)
        for pattern in patterns:
            for key, value in pattern.items():
                if isinstance(value, (int, float)):  # Consider only numerical attributes
                    attributes[key].append(value)

        # Calculate correlations
        for key1 in attributes:
            for key2 in attributes:
                if key1 != key2:
                    correlation_key = f"{key1}_vs_{key2}"
                    if correlation_key not in correlations and f"{key2}_vs_{key1}" not in correlations:
                        correlation = np.corrcoef(attributes[key1], attributes[key2])[0, 1]
                        correlations[correlation_key] = correlation

        return correlations

    @staticmethod
    def analyze_pattern_evolution(pattern_registry: AdvancedPatternRegistry) -> dict:
        """Analyze the evolution of patterns over time."""
        evolution_data = pattern_registry.analyze_pattern_evolution()
        analysis = {}

        for pattern_evolution in evolution_data:
            pattern_id = pattern_evolution['pattern_id']
            strength_changes = pattern_evolution['strength_changes']
            coherence_changes = pattern_evolution['coherence_changes']

            if len(strength_changes) > 1:
                # Calculate average change per step
                avg_strength_change = np.mean(np.diff([s[1] for s in strength_changes]))
                avg_coherence_change = np.mean(np.diff([c[1] for c in coherence_changes]))
                
                analysis[pattern_id] = {
                    'avg_strength_change': avg_strength_change,
                    'avg_coherence_change': avg_coherence_change
                }

        return analysis
    
    @staticmethod
    def detect_anomalies(patterns: list, threshold: float) -> list:
      """
      Detect anomalies in patterns based on a given threshold.
      Anomalies are defined as patterns whose strength or coherence is 
      significantly different from the average.
      """
      strengths = [pattern.get('strength', 0) for pattern in patterns]
      coherences = [pattern.get('coherence', 0) for pattern in patterns]
      
      avg_strength = np.mean(strengths)
      std_strength = np.std(strengths)
      avg_coherence = np.mean(coherences)
      std_coherence = np.std(coherences)
      
      anomalies = []
      for pattern in patterns:
          is_anomalous = False
          reason = ""

          # Check for strength anomalies
          if abs(pattern.get('strength', 0) - avg_strength) > threshold * std_strength:
              is_anomalous = True
              reason += f"Strength anomaly (strength={pattern.get('strength', 0):.2f}, avg={avg_strength:.2f}, std={std_strength:.2f}); "

          # Check for coherence anomalies
          if abs(pattern.get('coherence', 0) - avg_coherence) > threshold * std_coherence:
              is_anomalous = True
              reason += f"Coherence anomaly (coherence={pattern.get('coherence', 0):.2f}, avg={avg_coherence:.2f}, std={std_coherence:.2f}); "

          if is_anomalous:
              anomalies.append({
                  'pattern': pattern,
                  'reason': reason.strip()
              })
              
      return anomalies

utils/system_helpers.py

import numpy as np

class SystemHelpers:

    @staticmethod
    def normalize_data(data: np.ndarray) -> np.ndarray:
        """Normalize data to a range between 0 and 1."""
        min_val = np.min(data)
        max_val = np.max(data)
        if max_val - min_val == 0:
            return np.zeros_like(data)
        return (data - min_val) / (max_val - min_val)

    @staticmethod
    def scale_data(data: np.ndarray, new_min: float, new_max: float) -> np.ndarray:
        """Scale data to a new range."""
        min_val = np.min(data)
        max_val = np.max(data)
        if max_val - min_val == 0:
          return np.full_like(data, new_min)
        scaled_data = (data - min_val) / (max_val - min_val)  # Normalize to 0-1
        return scaled_data * (new_max - new_min) + new_min  # Scale to new range

    @staticmethod
    def apply_fft(data: np.ndarray) -> np.ndarray:
        """Apply Fast Fourier Transform to the data."""
        return np.fft.fft(data)

    @staticmethod
    def apply_inverse_fft(data: np.ndarray) -> np.ndarray:
        """Apply Inverse Fast Fourier Transform to the data."""
        return np.fft.ifft(data)

    @staticmethod
    def calculate_cross_correlation(data1: np.ndarray, data2: np.ndarray) -> np.ndarray:
        """Calculate the cross-correlation between two data sets."""
        return np.correlate(data1, data2, mode='full')
    
    @staticmethod
    def detect_outliers(data: np.ndarray, threshold: float = 3.0) -> list:
      """
      Detect outliers in the data using z-score.
      
      Args:
          data: Input data as a NumPy array.
          threshold: Z-score threshold for outlier detection.
          
      Returns:
          A list of indices where outliers are detected.
      """
      z_scores = np.abs((data - np.mean(data)) / np.std(data))
      outlier_indices = np.where(z_scores > threshold)[0]
      return outlier_indices.tolist()

import time
import json
import os

class DataStorage:
    def __init__(self, max_capacity: int = 10000, storage_directory: str = "data_storage"):
        self.data = []
        self.max_capacity = max_capacity
        self.storage_directory = storage_directory

        # Ensure storage directory exists
        if not os.path.exists(self.storage_directory):
            os.makedirs(self.storage_directory)

    def store_data(self, data_point: dict):
        """Store a data point with a timestamp."""
        timestamp = time.time()
        data_point_with_timestamp = {'timestamp': timestamp, 'data': data_point}
        self.data.append(data_point_with_timestamp)

        # Save to file (append mode)
        file_path = os.path.join(self.storage_directory, 'data.json')
        with open(file_path, 'a') as f:
            json.dump(data_point_with_timestamp, f)
            f.write('\n')  # Add a newline for each data point

        # Check capacity and remove oldest data if necessary
        if len(self.data) > self.max_capacity:
            self.data.pop(0)

    def retrieve_data(self, start_time, end_time) -> list:
        """Retrieve data within a specific time range."""
        retrieved_data = []
        for item in self.data:
            if start_time <= item['timestamp'] <= end_time:
                retrieved_data.append(item['data'])
        return retrieved_data

    def load_data_from_file(self, file_path: str = None):
        """Load data from a JSON file."""
        if file_path is None:
            file_path = os.path.join(self.storage_directory, 'data.json')
        
        if not os.path.exists(file_path):
            print(f"File not found: {file_path}")
            return

        try:
            with open(file_path, 'r') as f:
                for line in f:
                    try:
                        data_point = json.loads(line.strip())
                        self.data.append(data_point)
                    except json.JSONDecodeError as e:
                        print(f"Error decoding JSON: {e}")
        except Exception as e:
            print(f"Error reading file {file_path}: {e}")

        # Maintain capacity after loading
        if len(self.data) > self.max_capacity:
            self.data = self.data[-self.max_capacity:]

    def clear_data(self):
        """Clear all stored data."""
        self.data = []

        # Clear the data file
        file_path = os.path.join(self.storage_directory, 'data.json')
        if os.path.exists(file_path):
            with open(file_path, 'w') as f:
                f.write('')  # Overwrite with empty content
                
    def get_state(self) -> dict:
        """Returns the current state of the data storage."""
        return {
            'max_capacity': self.max_capacity,
            'current_size': len(self.data),
            'storage_directory': self.storage_directory
        }

system/tests/unit/test_quantum_engine.py
Python

import unittest
import numpy as np
from kaleidoscope.core.quantum_engine import QuantumEngine

class TestQuantumEngine(unittest.TestCase):

    def setUp(self):
        self.dimensions = 4
        self.banks_per_dimension = 2
        self.engine = QuantumEngine(self.dimensions, self.banks_per_dimension)

    def test_initialization(self):
        self.assertEqual(self.engine.dimensions, self.dimensions)
        self.assertEqual(self.engine.banks_per_dimension, self.banks_per_dimension)
        self.assertEqual(self.engine.quantum_states.shape, (self.dimensions, self.banks_per_dimension))
        self.assertEqual(len(self.engine.entanglement_graph.nodes), self.dimensions)

    def test_apply_quantum_transformations(self):
        data = np.random.rand(self.dimensions, self.banks_per_dimension)
        transformed_data = self.engine._apply_quantum_transformations(data)
        self.assertEqual(transformed_data.shape, data.shape)
        self.assertTrue(np.all(transformed_data.dtype == np.complex128))

    def test_hadamard_transform(self):
        state = 0.5 + 0.5j  # Example state
        transformed_state = self.engine._hadamard_transform(state)
        # Check if the transformation is approximately correct
        expected_state = (0.5 + 0.5j) / np.sqrt(2) + (0.5 - 0.5j) / np.sqrt(2)
        self.assertAlmostEqual(transformed_state, expected_state)

    def test_apply_phase_rotation(self):
        state = 0.5 + 0.5j  # Example state
        dim = 0
        # Add a neighbor to create entanglement
        self.engine.entanglement_graph.add_edge(dim, 1)
        self.engine.resonance_matrix[dim, 1] = 0.5 + 0.5j
        rotated_state = self.engine._apply_phase_rotation(state, dim)
        self.assertNotEqual(rotated_state, state)  # State should change due to phase rotation

    def test_calculate_resonance(self):
        data = np.random.rand(self.dimensions, self.banks_per_dimension) + 1j * np.random.rand(self.dimensions, self.banks_per_dimension)
        resonance_effects = self.engine._calculate_resonance(data)
        self.assertEqual(resonance_effects.shape, data.shape)
        self.assertTrue(np.all(resonance_effects.dtype == np.complex128))
    
    def test_get_quantum_state_metrics(self):
        metrics = self.engine.get_quantum_state_metrics()
        self.assertIn('entanglement_entropy', metrics)
        self.assertIn('average_resonance', metrics)
        self.assertIn('state_purity', metrics)
        self.assertTrue(isinstance(metrics['entanglement_entropy'], float))
        self.assertTrue(isinstance(metrics['average_resonance'], float))
        self.assertTrue(isinstance(metrics['state_purity'], float))

    def test_update_entanglement(self):
        # Initially, there should be some entanglement
        initial_edges = self.engine.entanglement_graph.number_of_edges()
        
        # Set a high resonance value to ensure entanglement
        self.engine.resonance_matrix.fill(1.0 + 1.0j)
        self.engine.update_entanglement(threshold=0.5)
        edges_after_high_resonance = self.engine.entanglement_graph.number_of_edges()
        self.assertGreater(edges_after_high_resonance, initial_edges)

        # Set a low resonance value to remove entanglement
        self.engine.resonance_matrix.fill(0.0 + 0.0j)
        self.engine.update_entanglement(threshold=0.5)
        edges_after_low_resonance = self.engine.entanglement_graph.number_of_edges()
        self.assertEqual(edges_after_low_resonance, 0)
    
if __name__ == '__main__':
    unittest.main()

system/tests/unit/test_resonance_manager.py
Python

import unittest
import numpy as np
from kaleidoscope.core.resonance_manager import ResonanceManager

class TestResonanceManager(unittest.TestCase):

    def setUp(self):
        self.dimensions = 3
        self.manager = ResonanceManager(self.dimensions)

    def test_initialization(self):
        self.assertEqual(self.manager.dimensions, self.dimensions)
        self.assertEqual(self.manager.resonance_fields.shape, (self.dimensions, 360))
        self.assertEqual(len(self.manager.resonance_graph.nodes), self.dimensions)

    def test_update_resonance_fields(self):
        data = np.random.rand(self.dimensions, 360) + 1j * np.random.rand(self.dimensions, 360)
        self.manager.update_resonance_fields(data)
        for dim in range(self.dimensions):
            self.assertTrue(np.allclose(self.manager.resonance_fields[dim], self.manager._calculate_resonance_field(data[dim], dim)))

    def test_calculate_resonance_field(self):
        data = np.random.rand(360) + 1j * np.random.rand(360)
        dim = 0
        field = self.manager._calculate_resonance_field(data, dim)
        self.assertEqual(field.shape, (360,))
        self.assertTrue(np.all(field.dtype == np.complex128))

    def test_analyze_resonance_patterns(self):
        # Create a known pattern in the resonance field
        field = np.zeros(360, dtype=np.complex128)
        field[45] = 10 + 0j  # Strong peak at 45 degrees
        self.manager.resonance_fields[0] = field

        patterns = self.manager.analyze_resonance_patterns()
        self.assertTrue(any(p['angle'] == 45 and p['dimension'] == 0 for p in patterns))

    def test_find_peaks(self):
        field = np.zeros(360, dtype=np.complex128)
        field[30] = 5 + 0j
        field[180] = 10 + 0j
        peaks = self.manager._find_peaks(field)
        peak_angles = [peak['angle'] for peak in peaks]
        self.assertIn(30, peak_angles)
        self.assertIn(180, peak_angles)

    def test_update_resonance_graph(self):
        # Create some artificial patterns
        patterns = [
            {'dimension': 0, 'angle': 30, 'magnitude': 1, 'phase': 0},
            {'dimension': 1, 'angle': 30, 'magnitude': 0.9, 'phase': 0.1}
        ]
        self.manager.update_resonance_graph(patterns)
        self.assertTrue(self.manager.resonance_graph.has_edge(0, 1))

    def test_calculate_resonance_strength(self):
        node1 = {'field': np.ones(360, dtype=np.complex128)}
        node2 = {'field': np.ones(360, dtype=np.complex128)}
        strength = self.manager._calculate_resonance_strength(node1, node2)
        self.assertAlmostEqual(strength, 1.0) 

    def test_get_resonance_metrics(self):
        metrics = self.manager.get_resonance_metrics()
        self.assertIn('average_field_strength', metrics)
        self.assertIn('resonance_connectivity', metrics)
        self.assertIn('field_entropy', metrics)
        self.assertTrue(isinstance(metrics['average_field_strength'], float))
        self.assertTrue(isinstance(metrics['resonance_connectivity'], float))
        self.assertTrue(isinstance(metrics['field_entropy'], float))

if __name__ == '__main__':
    unittest.main()

system/tests/unit/test_quantum_processor.py
Python

import unittest
import numpy as np
from kaleidoscope.core.quantum_engine import QuantumEngine
from kaleidoscope.core.resonance_manager import ResonanceManager
from kaleidoscope.core.quantum_processor import QuantumProcessor

class TestQuantumProcessor(unittest.TestCase):

    def setUp(self):
        self.dimensions = 3
        self.banks_per_dimension = 2
        self.quantum_engine = QuantumEngine(self.dimensions, self.banks_per_dimension)
        self.resonance_manager = ResonanceManager(self.dimensions)
        self.processor = QuantumProcessor(self.quantum_engine, self.resonance_manager)

    def test_initialization(self):
        self.assertEqual(self.processor.quantum_engine.dimensions, self.dimensions)
        self.assertEqual(self.processor.resonance_manager.dimensions, self.dimensions)
        self.assertEqual(len(self.processor.insight_memory), 0)

    def test_process(self):
        data = np.random.rand(self.dimensions, self.banks_per_dimension)
        result = self.processor.process(data)
        self.assertIn('transformed_data', result)
        self.assertIn('patterns', result)
        self.assertIn('insights', result)
        self.assertTrue(len(result['patterns']) > 0)

    def test_generate_insights(self):
        patterns = [
            {'dimension': 0, 'angle': 45, 'magnitude': 0.9, 'phase': 0.1},
            {'dimension': 1, 'angle': 225, 'magnitude': 0.8, 'phase': 0.2}
        ]
        insights = self.processor.generate_insights(patterns)
        self.assertTrue(len(insights) > 0)

    def test_adapt_processing(self):
        insights = [{
            'type': 'interdimensional_resonance',
            'dimensions': (0, 1),
            'angles': (45, 225),
            'magnitudes': (0.9, 0.8),
            'phases': (0.1, 0.2)
        }]
        initial_resonance = self.quantum_engine.resonance_matrix[0, 1]
        self.processor.adapt_processing(insights)
        updated_resonance = self.quantum_engine.resonance_matrix[0, 1]
        self.assertNotEqual(initial_resonance, updated_resonance)
        
    def test_get_combined_metrics(self):
        metrics = self.processor.get_combined_metrics()
        self.assertIn('quantum', metrics)
        self.assertIn('resonance', metrics)
        self.assertIn('pattern_history_length', metrics)
        self.assertIn('insight_memory_length', metrics)
        self.assertTrue(isinstance(metrics['quantum']['entanglement_entropy'], float))
        self.assertTrue(isinstance(metrics['resonance']['average_field_strength'], float))

system/tests/unit/test_resonance.py
Python

import unittest
import numpy as np
from kaleidoscope.quantum.resonance import Resonance

class TestResonance(unittest.TestCase):

    def setUp(self):
        self.dimension = 1
        self.frequency = 1.0
        self.amplitude = 0.5
        self.phase = 0.0
        self.resonance = Resonance(self.dimension, self.frequency, self.amplitude, self.phase)

    def test_initialization(self):
        self.assertEqual(self.resonance.dimension, self.dimension)
        self.assertEqual(self.resonance.frequency, self.frequency)
        self.assertEqual(self.resonance.amplitude, self.amplitude)
        self.assertEqual(self.resonance.phase, self.phase)
        self.assertEqual(self.resonance.connections, {})

    def test_connect(self):
        other_resonance = Resonance(2, 2.0, 0.75, 0.5)
        strength = 0.8
        self.resonance.connect(other_resonance, strength)
        self.assertIn(other_resonance, self.resonance.connections)
        self.assertEqual(self.resonance.connections[other_resonance], strength)
        self.assertIn(self.resonance, other_resonance.connections)
        self.assertEqual(other_resonance.connections[self.resonance], strength)

    def test_disconnect(self):
        other_resonance = Resonance(2, 2.0, 0.75, 0.5)
        strength = 0.8
        self.resonance.connect(other_resonance, strength)
        self.resonance.disconnect(other_resonance)
        self.assertNotIn(other_resonance, self.resonance.connections)
        self.assertNotIn(self.resonance, other_resonance.connections)

    def test_get_connection_strength(self):
        other_resonance = Resonance(2, 2.0, 0.75, 0.5)
        strength = 0.8
        self.resonance.connect(other_resonance, strength)
        self.assertEqual(self.resonance.get_connection_strength(other_resonance), strength)
        self.assertEqual(self.resonance.get_connection_strength(Resonance(3, 1.0, 0.5, 0.0)), 0)

    def test_calculate_resonance_field(self):
        data = np.random.rand(360) + 1j * np.random.rand(360)
        field = self.resonance.calculate_resonance_field(data)
        self.assertEqual(field.shape, (360,))
        self.assertTrue(np.all(field.dtype == np.complex128))

    def test_get_state(self):
        state = self.resonance.get_state()
        self.assertEqual(state['dimension'], self.dimension)
        self.assertEqual(state['frequency'], self.frequency)
        self.assertEqual(state['amplitude'], self.amplitude)
        self.assertEqual(state['phase'], self.phase)
        self.assertEqual(state['connections'], {})
        
if __name__ == '__main__':
    unittest.main()

system/tests/unit/test_entanglement.py
Python

import unittest
import numpy as np
import networkx as nx
from kaleidoscope.quantum.entanglement import EntanglementManager

class TestEntanglementManager(unittest.TestCase):

    def setUp(self):
        self.dimensions = 3
        self.manager = EntanglementManager(self.dimensions)

    def test_initialization(self):
        self.assertEqual(self.manager.dimensions, self.dimensions)
        self.assertEqual(len(self.manager.entanglement_graph.nodes), self.dimensions)
        self.assertTrue(np.all(self.manager.entanglement_matrix == 0))

    def test_update_entanglement(self):
        patterns = [
            {'type': 'interdimensional_resonance', 'dimensions': (0, 1), 'magnitudes': (0.9, 0.8), 'phases': (0.1, 0.2)},
            {'type': 'interdimensional_resonance', 'dimensions': (1, 2), 'magnitudes': (0.7, 0.6), 'phases': (0.3, 0.4)}
        ]
        self.manager.update_entanglement(patterns)
        self.assertTrue(self.manager.entanglement_graph.has_edge(0, 1))
        self.assertTrue(self.manager.entanglement_graph.has_edge(1, 2))
        self.assertNotEqual(self.manager.entanglement_matrix[0, 1], 0)

    def test_calculate_entanglement_strength(self):
        self.manager.entanglement_matrix[0, 1] = 0.5 + 0.5j
        strength = self.manager.calculate_entanglement_strength(0, 1)
        self.assertAlmostEqual(strength, np.abs(0.5 + 0.5j))

    def test_get_entangled_pairs(self):
        self.manager.entanglement_graph.add_edge(0, 1)
        self.manager.entanglement_graph.add_edge(1, 2)
        pairs = self.manager.get_entangled_pairs()
        self.assertIn((0, 1), pairs)
        self.assertIn((1, 2), pairs)

    def test_calculate_global_entanglement(self):
        self.manager.entanglement_matrix[0, 1] = 0.5 + 0.5j
        self.manager.entanglement_matrix[1, 2] = 0.6 + 0.6j
        global_entanglement = self.manager.calculate_global_entanglement()
        self.assertGreater(global_entanglement, 0)

    def test_get_entanglement_entropy(self):
        # Add some entanglement to the graph
        self.manager.entanglement_graph.add_edge(0, 1)
        self.manager.entanglement_graph.add_edge(0, 2)
        entropy = self.manager.get_entanglement_entropy(0)
        self.assertTrue(isinstance(entropy, float))
        self.assertGreaterEqual(entropy, 0)

    def test_get_state(self):
        self.manager.entanglement_matrix[0, 1] = 0.5 + 0.5j
        self.manager.entanglement_graph.add_edge(0, 1)
        state = self.manager.get_state()
        self.assertIn('entanglement_graph', state)
        self.assertIn('entanglement_matrix', state)
        self.assertIn('global_entanglement', state)
        self.assertEqual(state['entanglement_matrix'][0][1], 0.5 + 0.5j)
        self.assertTrue(len(state['entanglement_graph']) > 0)
        
if __name__ == '__main__':
    unittest.main()

import unittest
import numpy as np
from kaleidoscope.quantum.harmonics import HarmonicOscillator

class TestHarmonicOscillator(unittest.TestCase):

    def setUp(self):
        self.frequency = 1.0
        self.amplitude = 0.5
        self.phase = 0.0
        self.dimension = 1
        self.oscillator = HarmonicOscillator(self.frequency, self.amplitude, self.phase, self.dimension)

    def test_initialization(self):
        self.assertEqual(self.oscillator.frequency, self.frequency)
        self.assertEqual(self.oscillator.amplitude, self.amplitude)
        self.assertEqual(self.oscillator.phase, self.phase)
        self.assertEqual(self.oscillator.dimension, self.dimension)
        self.assertEqual(self.oscillator.energy, self.amplitude ** 2)

    def test_resonate(self):
        external_field = np.ones(360, dtype=np.complex128)
        initial_amplitude = self.oscillator.amplitude
        self.oscillator.resonate(external_field)
        self.assertNotEqual(self.oscillator.amplitude, initial_amplitude)

    def test_get_state(self):
        state = self.oscillator.get_state()
        self.assertEqual(state['frequency'], self.frequency)
        self.assertEqual(state['amplitude'], self.amplitude)
        self.assertEqual(state['phase'], self.phase)
        self.assertEqual(state['dimension'], self.dimension)
        self.assertEqual(state['energy'], self.amplitude ** 2)

if __name__ == '__main__':
    unittest.main()

system/tests/unit/test_banks.py
Python

import unittest
import numpy as np
from kaleidoscope.memory.banks import MemoryBank

class TestMemoryBank(unittest.TestCase):

    def setUp(self):
        self.capacity = 10
        self.dimensions = 3
        self.bank = MemoryBank(self.capacity, self.dimensions)

    def test_initialization(self):
        self.assertEqual(self.bank.capacity, self.capacity)
        self.assertEqual(self.bank.dimensions, self.dimensions)
        self.assertEqual(self.bank.data.shape, (self.capacity, self.dimensions))
        self.assertEqual(self.bank.head, 0)

    def test_store(self):
        data_vector = np.array([1 + 1j, 2 + 2j, 3 + 3j])
        self.bank.store(data_vector)
        self.assertEqual(self.bank.head, 1)
        self.assertTrue(np.allclose(self.bank.data[0], data_vector))

    def test_retrieve(self):
        data_vector = np.array([1 + 1j, 2 + 2j, 3 + 3j])
        self.bank.store(data_vector)
        retrieved_vector = self.bank.retrieve(0)
        self.assertTrue(np.allclose(retrieved_vector, data_vector))

    def test_get_recent(self):
        for i in range(self.capacity):
            self.bank.store(np.ones(self.dimensions, dtype=np.complex128) * i)
        recent_data = self.bank.get_recent(5)
        self.assertEqual(recent_data.shape, (5, self.dimensions))
        self.assertTrue(np.allclose(recent_data[0], np.ones(self.dimensions) * 5))
        self.assertTrue(np.allclose(recent_data[-1], np.ones(self.dimensions) * 9))

    def test_store_exceed_capacity(self):
        for i in range(self.capacity + 5):
            self.bank.store(np.ones(self.dimensions, dtype=np.complex128) * i)
        self.assertEqual(self.bank.head, 5)
        self.assertTrue(np.allclose(self.bank.retrieve(0), np.ones(self.dimensions) * 10))

    def test_retrieve_invalid_index(self):
        with self.assertRaises(IndexError):
            self.bank.retrieve(-1)
        with self.assertRaises(IndexError):
            self.bank.retrieve(self.capacity)

    def test_get_recent_more_than_capacity(self):
        with self.assertRaises(ValueError):
            self.bank.get_recent(self.capacity + 1)
            
    def test_get_state(self):
        for i in range(5):
            self.bank.store(np.ones(self.dimensions, dtype=np.complex128) * i)
        state = self.bank.get_state()
        self.assertEqual(state['capacity'], self.capacity)
        self.assertEqual(state['dimensions'], self.dimensions)
        self.assertEqual(state['head'], 5)
        self.assertEqual(state['fill_level'], 0.6)  # 6 out of 10 slots filled (head is at 5, indicating 5+1 elements stored)

if __name__ == '__main__':
    unittest.main()

system/tests/unit/test_pattern_storage.py
Python

import unittest
from kaleidoscope.memory.pattern_storage import PatternStorage

class TestPatternStorage(unittest.TestCase):

    def setUp(self):
        self.storage = PatternStorage(max_patterns=5)

    def test_initialization(self):
        self.assertEqual(self.storage.max_patterns, 5)
        self.assertEqual(self.storage.pattern_count, 0)
        self.assertEqual(len(self.storage.patterns), 0)

    def test_store_pattern(self):
        pattern = {'type': 'test', 'magnitude': 0.5, 'phase': 0.1}
        self.storage.store_pattern(pattern)
        self.assertEqual(self.storage.pattern_count, 1)
        self.assertIn(pattern, self.storage.patterns)

    def test_store_pattern_exceed_capacity(self):
        for i in range(6):
            self.storage.store_pattern({'type': 'test', 'magnitude': i, 'phase': 0.1})
        self.assertEqual(self.storage.pattern_count, 5)
        self.assertEqual(len(self.storage.patterns), 5)

    def test_retrieve_similar_patterns(self):
        pattern1 = {'type': 'test', 'magnitude': 0.5, 'phase': 0.1}
        pattern2 = {'type': 'test', 'magnitude': 0.6, 'phase': 0.2}
        pattern3 = {'type': 'other', 'magnitude': 0.8, 'phase': 0.5}
        self.storage.store_pattern(pattern1)
        self.storage.store_pattern(pattern2)
        self.storage.store_pattern(pattern3)

        similar_patterns = self.storage.retrieve_similar_patterns(pattern1, 0.8)
        self.assertIn(pattern1, similar_patterns)
        self.assertIn(pattern2, similar_patterns)
        self.assertNotIn(pattern3, similar_patterns)

    def test_calculate_similarity(self):
        pattern1 = {'type': 'test', 'magnitude': 0.5, 'phase': 0.1}
        pattern2 = {'type': 'test', 'magnitude': 0.6, 'phase': 0.2}
        pattern3 = {'type': 'other', 'magnitude': 0.8, 'phase': 0.5}
        similarity12 = self.storage._calculate_similarity(pattern1, pattern2)
        similarity13 = self.storage._calculate_similarity(pattern1, pattern3)
        self.assertGreater(similarity12, similarity13)
        self.assertTrue(0 <= similarity12 <= 1)
        self.assertTrue(0 <= similarity13 <= 1)

    def test_extract_features(self):
        pattern = {'type': 'test', 'magnitude': 0.5, 'phase': 0.1}
        features = self.storage._extract_features(pattern)
        self.assertEqual(len(features), 3)  # Assuming type, magnitude, and phase are used as features
        self.assertIn(0.5, features)
        self.assertIn(0.1, features)

    def test_get_state(self):
        pattern = {'type': 'test', 'magnitude': 0.5, 'phase': 0.1, 'timestamp': '2023-01-01T00:00:00'}
        self.storage.store_pattern(pattern)
        state = self.storage.get_state()
        self.assertEqual(state['max_patterns'], 5)
        self.assertEqual(state['pattern_count'], 1)
        self.assertEqual(state['oldest_pattern_timestamp'], '2023-01-01T00:00:00')

if __name__ == '__main__':
    unittest.main()

import unittest
import networkx as nx
from kaleidoscope.processing.flow_paths import FlowPathManager

class TestFlowPathManager(unittest.TestCase):

    def setUp(self):
        self.dimensions = 3
        self.manager = FlowPathManager(self.dimensions)

    def test_initialization(self):
        self.assertEqual(self.manager.dimensions, self.dimensions)
        self.assertEqual(len(self.manager.flow_graph.nodes), self.dimensions)
        self.assertEqual(len(self.manager.flow_graph.edges), 0)

    def test_update_flow_paths(self):
        patterns = [
            {'type': 'interdimensional_resonance', 'dimensions': (0, 1), 'magnitudes': (0.8, 0.7)},
            {'type': 'interdimensional_resonance', 'dimensions': (1, 2), 'magnitudes': (0.6, 0.5)}
        ]
        self.manager.update_flow_paths(patterns)
        self.assertTrue(self.manager.flow_graph.has_edge(0, 1))
        self.assertTrue(self.manager.flow_graph.has_edge(1, 2))
        self.assertAlmostEqual(self.manager.flow_graph[0][1]['weight'], 0.75)
        self.assertAlmostEqual(self.manager.flow_graph[1][2]['weight'], 0.55)

    def test_calculate_flow_strength(self):
        self.manager.flow_graph.add_edge(0, 1, weight=0.8)
        self.manager.flow_graph.add_edge(1, 2, weight=0.6)
        strength = self.manager.calculate_flow_strength([0, 1, 2])
        self.assertAlmostEqual(strength, 1.4)

    def test_find_strongest_flow_paths(self):
        self.manager.flow_graph.add_edge(0, 1, weight=0.8)
        self.manager.flow_graph.add_edge(1, 2, weight=0.6)
        self.manager.flow_graph.add_edge(0, 2, weight=0.9)  # Stronger direct path

        strongest_paths = self.manager.find_strongest_flow_paths(top_k=2)
        self.assertEqual(len(strongest_paths), 2)
        self.assertEqual(strongest_paths[0][0], [0, 2])  # Strongest path should be the direct path
        self.assertAlmostEqual(strongest_paths[0][1], 0.9)

    def test_get_flow_path_distribution(self):
        self.manager.flow_graph.add_edge(0, 1, weight=0.8)
        self.manager.flow_graph.add_edge(1, 2, weight=0.6)
        distribution = self.manager.get_flow_path_distribution()
        self.assertIn('mean', distribution)
        self.assertIn('std', distribution)
        self.assertIn('histogram', distribution)
        self.assertEqual(len(distribution['histogram']), 10)  # Default number of bins

    def test_get_state(self):
        self.manager.flow_graph.add_edge(0, 1, weight=0.8)
        state = self.manager.get_state()
        self.assertEqual(state['dimensions'], self.dimensions)
        self.assertTrue(len(state['flow_graph_edges']) > 0)
        self.assertIn('flow_path_distribution', state)

if __name__ == '__main__':
    unittest.main()

import unittest
from unittest.mock import MagicMock
from kaleidoscope.processing.pattern_synthesis import PatternSynthesizer

class TestPatternSynthesizer(unittest.TestCase):

    def setUp(self):
        self.mock_resonance_manager = MagicMock()
        self.synthesizer = PatternSynthesizer(self.mock_resonance_manager)

    def test_initialization(self):
        self.assertEqual(self.synthesizer.resonance_manager, self.mock_resonance_manager)
        self.assertEqual(len(self.synthesizer.synthesized_patterns), 0)

    def test_synthesize_patterns(self):
        patterns = [
            {'dimension': 0, 'angle': 30, 'magnitude': 0.8, 'phase': 0.1},
            {'dimension': 1, 'angle': 35, 'magnitude': 0.7, 'phase': 0.2},
            {'dimension': 2, 'angle': 150, 'magnitude': 0.6, 'phase': 0.5}
        ]
        # Mock the resonance graph to have nodes for each dimension
        self.mock_resonance_manager.resonance_graph.nodes = MagicMock(return_value=[0, 1, 2])
        
        synthesized_patterns = self.synthesizer.synthesize_patterns(patterns)
        
        self.assertTrue(len(synthesized_patterns) > 0)
        self.assertEqual(synthesized_patterns[0]['type'], 'synthesized_resonance')

    def test_calculate_pattern_coherence(self):
        pattern1 = {'phase': 0.1}
        pattern2 = {'phase': 0.3}
        coherence = self.synthesizer._calculate_pattern_coherence(pattern1, pattern2)
        self.assertGreaterEqual(coherence, -1.0)
        self.assertLessEqual(coherence, 1.0)

    def test_get_synthesized_pattern_types(self):
        self.synthesizer.synthesized_patterns = [
            {'type': 'type1'},
            {'type': 'type2'},
            {'type': 'type1'}
        ]
        types = self.synthesizer.get_synthesized_pattern_types()
        self.assertIn('type1', types)
        self.assertIn('type2', types)
        self.assertEqual(len(types), 2)

    def test_get_state(self):
        self.synthesizer.synthesized_patterns = [{'type': 'test_pattern', 'timestamp': '2023-01-01T00:00:00'}]
        state = self.synthesizer.get_state()
        self.assertEqual(state['synthesized_patterns_count'], 1)
        self.assertEqual(len(state['last_synthesized_patterns']), 1)

if __name__ == '__main__':
    unittest.main()

system/tests/unit/test_dimensional_processing.py
Python

import unittest
import numpy as np
from kaleidoscope.processing.dimensional_processing import DimensionalProcessor

class TestDimensionalProcessor(unittest.TestCase):

    def setUp(self):
        self.dimensions = 3
        self.processor = DimensionalProcessor(self.dimensions)

    def test_initialization(self):
        self.assertEqual(self.processor.dimensions, self.dimensions)
        self.assertEqual(len(self.processor.transformation_matrices), self.dimensions)
        for dim in range(self.dimensions):
            self.assertTrue(np.array_equal(self.processor.transformation_matrices[dim], np.eye(dim + 1)))

    def test_process_dimension(self):
        data = np.random.rand(2)
        dim = 1
        transformed_data = self.processor.process_dimension(dim, data)
        self.assertEqual(transformed_data.shape, (dim + 1,))

    def test_update_transformation_matrix(self):
        new_matrix = np.array([[1, 2], [3, 4]])
        dim = 1
        self.processor.update_transformation_matrix(dim, new_matrix)
        self.assertTrue(np.array_equal(self.processor.transformation_matrices[dim], new_matrix))

    def test_get_transformation_matrix(self):
        dim = 1
        matrix = self.processor.get_transformation_matrix(dim)
        self.assertTrue(np.array_equal(matrix, np.eye(dim + 1)))

    def test_process_dimension_invalid_dimension(self):
        with self.assertRaises(ValueError):
            self.processor.process_dimension(self.dimensions + 1, np.random.rand(2))

    def test_update_transformation_matrix_invalid_shape(self):
        with self.assertRaises(ValueError):
            self.processor.update_transformation_matrix(0, np.array([[1, 2]]))
            
    def test_get_state(self):
        state = self.processor.get_state()
        self.assertEqual(state['dimensions'], self.dimensions)
        self.assertEqual(len(state['transformation_matrices']), self.dimensions)
        for dim in range(self.dimensions):
            self.assertTrue(np.array_equal(state['transformation_matrices'][str(dim)], np.eye(dim + 1).tolist()))

if __name__ == '__main__':
    unittest.main()

system/tests/unit/test_perspective_processor.py
