system/c_backend/core/bank_operations.c
C

#include <stdio.h>
#include <stdlib.h>
#include <string.h>
#include <math.h>
#include <complex.h>
#include "bank_operations.h"

// Function to initialize a memory bank
void initialize_bank(MemoryBank *bank, int capacity, int dimensions) {
    bank->capacity = capacity;
    bank->dimensions = dimensions;
    bank->data = (double complex *)malloc(capacity * dimensions * sizeof(double complex));
    bank->head = 0;
    if (bank->data == NULL) {
        fprintf(stderr, "Memory allocation failed for bank data\n");
        exit(EXIT_FAILURE);
    }
}

// Function to store a data vector in the memory bank
void store_data(MemoryBank *bank, double complex *data_vector) {
    int index = (bank->head * bank->dimensions);
    memcpy(&bank->data[index], data_vector, bank->dimensions * sizeof(double complex));
    bank->head = (bank->head + 1) % bank->capacity;
}

// Function to retrieve a data vector from the memory bank
void retrieve_data(MemoryBank *bank, int index, double complex *data_vector) {
    if (index < 0 || index >= bank->capacity) {
        fprintf(stderr, "Index out of range for memory bank retrieval\n");
        exit(EXIT_FAILURE);
    }
    int data_index = (index * bank->dimensions);
    memcpy(data_vector, &bank->data[data_index], bank->dimensions * sizeof(double complex));
}

// Function to get the n most recently stored data vectors
void get_recent_data(MemoryBank *bank, int n, double complex *recent_data) {
    if (n > bank->capacity) {
        fprintf(stderr, "Cannot retrieve more elements than the capacity of the memory bank\n");
        exit(EXIT_FAILURE);
    }
    int start = (bank->head - n + bank->capacity) % bank->capacity;
    for (int i = 0; i < n; i++) {
        int index = ((start + i) % bank->capacity) * bank->dimensions;
        memcpy(&recent_data[i * bank->dimensions], &bank->data[index], bank->dimensions * sizeof(double complex));
    }
}

// Function to free the memory allocated to the bank
void free_bank(MemoryBank *bank) {
    free(bank->data);
    bank->data = NULL;
}

system/c_backend/core/quantum_operations.c
C

#include <stdio.h>
#include <stdlib.h>
#include <math.h>
#include <complex.h>
#include "quantum_operations.h"

// Function to apply the Hadamard transformation to a quantum state
void hadamard_transform(double complex *state) {
    double complex temp = *state;
    *state = (temp + (1 - temp)) / sqrt(2);
}

// Function to apply a phase rotation to a quantum state
void phase_rotation(double complex *state, double phase) {
    *state *= cexp(I * phase);
}

// Function to calculate the density matrix for a quantum state
void calculate_density_matrix(double complex state, double complex density_matrix[2][2]) {
    density_matrix[0][0] = state * conj(state);
    density_matrix[0][1] = state * conj(1-state);
    density_matrix[1][0] = (1-state) * conj(state);
    density_matrix[1][1] = (1-state) * conj(1-state);
}

// Function to calculate the purity of a quantum state
double calculate_purity(double complex density_matrix[2][2]) {
    double complex trace = 0.0;
    for (int i = 0; i < 2; i++){
        for(int j = 0; j < 2; j++){
            trace += density_matrix[i][j] * density_matrix[j][i];
        }
    }
    return creal(trace);
}

// Function to simulate the measurement of a quantum state
int measure_state(double complex state) {
    double prob = complex_magnitude(state) * complex_magnitude(state);
    double random_val = (double)rand() / RAND_MAX;
    return (random_val < prob) ? 1 : 0;
}

system/c_backend/Makefile

CC = gcc
CFLAGS = -fPIC -Wall -Wextra -O3 -march=native -shared

# Directories
COREDIR = core
NODEDIR = node
MEMDIR = memory
INCLUDEDIR = include

# Source files
CORE_SOURCES = $(COREDIR)/core_processing.c $(COREDIR)/bank_operations.c $(COREDIR)/quantum_operations.c
NODE_SOURCES = $(NODEDIR)/node_operations.c $(NODEDIR)/energy_management.c
MEMORY_SOURCES = $(MEMDIR)/memory_management.c $(MEMDIR)/pattern_operations.c

# Object files
CORE_OBJECTS = $(CORE_SOURCES:.c=.o)
NODE_OBJECTS = $(NODE_SOURCES:.c=.o)
MEMORY_OBJECTS = $(MEMORY_SOURCES:.c=.o)

# Library names
CORE_LIB = kaleidoscope_core.so
NODE_LIB = node_operations.so
MEMORY_LIB = memory_operations.so

all: $(CORE_LIB) $(NODE_LIB) $(MEMORY_LIB)

$(CORE_LIB): $(CORE_OBJECTS)
	$(CC) $(CFLAGS) -o $@ $^

$(NODE_LIB): $(NODE_OBJECTS)
	$(CC) $(CFLAGS) -o $@ $^

$(MEMORY_LIB): $(MEMORY_OBJECTS)
	$(CC) $(CFLAGS) -o $@ $^

%.o: %.c
	$(CC) $(CFLAGS) -I$(INCLUDEDIR) -c $< -o $@

clean:
	rm -f $(COREDIR)/*.o $(NODEDIR)/*.o $(MEMDIR)/*.o $(CORE_LIB) $(NODE_LIB) $(MEMORY_LIB)

.PHONY: all clean

system/c_backend/node/node_operations.c
C

#include <stdio.h>
#include <stdlib.h>
#include <string.h>
#include "node_operations.h"

// Function to initialize a node
Node *initialize_node(const char *node_id, double capacity, const char *role, const char *ip_address, int port) {
    Node *node = (Node *)malloc(sizeof(Node));
    if (node == NULL) {
        fprintf(stderr, "Memory allocation failed for node\n");
        exit(EXIT_FAILURE);
    }

    // Allocate and copy data for string fields to avoid shallow copy issues
    node->node_id = strdup(node_id);
    node->role = strdup(role);
    node->ip_address = strdup(ip_address);

    if (node->node_id == NULL || node->role == NULL || node->ip_address == NULL) {
        fprintf(stderr, "Memory allocation failed for node fields\n");
        free(node->node_id);
        free(node->role);
        free(node->ip_address);
        free(node);
        exit(EXIT_FAILURE);
    }

    node->capacity = capacity;
    node->status = strdup("idle"); // Allocate memory for status string
    node->last_heartbeat = 0; // Initialize to 0 or a default value
    node->port = port;

    return node;
}

// Function to assign a task to a node
void assign_task(Node *node, const char *task_id) {
    // Assuming a simple task assignment mechanism for demonstration
    // In a real system, you might have a more complex task structure
    node->status = strdup("processing");
    // You can add the task_id to a list of tasks if needed
}

// Function to update the status of a node
void update_node_status(Node *node, const char *status) {
    free(node->status);  // Free the previously allocated status string
    node->status = strdup(status);
    if (node->status == NULL) {
        fprintf(stderr, "Memory allocation failed for node status\n");
        exit(EXIT_FAILURE);
    }
}

// Function to send a heartbeat from a node
void send_heartbeat(Node *node) {
    node->last_heartbeat = (unsigned long long)time(NULL); // Update with the current timestamp
}

// Function to free the memory allocated to a node
void free_node(Node *node) {
    free(node->node_id);
    free(node->role);
    free(node->ip_address);
    free(node->status);
    free(node);
}

system/c_backend/node/energy_management.c
C

#include <stdio.h>
#include <stdlib.h>
#include "energy_management.h"
#include "node_operations.h"

// Function to calculate the current energy usage of a node
double calculate_energy_usage(Node *node) {
    // Placeholder for energy usage calculation
    // In a real system, this might involve reading from hardware sensors or system monitors
    // For now, we'll simulate it based on node status and capacity
    if (strcmp(node->status, "idle") == 0) {
        return node->capacity * 0.1; // 10% usage when idle
    } else if (strcmp(node->status, "processing") == 0) {
        return node->capacity * 0.8; // 80% usage when processing
    } else {
        return 0.0; // No usage for unknown status
    }
}

// Function to set a node to energy-saving mode
void set_energy_saving_mode(Node *node, int enabled) {
    if (enabled) {
        update_node_status(node, "energy_saving");
        // Additional logic to reduce energy consumption can be added here
        printf("Node %s set to energy-saving mode\n", node->node_id);
    } else {
        update_node_status(node, "idle");
        // Logic to return to normal operation can be added here
        printf("Node %s returned to normal operation\n", node->node_id);
    }
}

// Function to get a list of nodes with low energy
void get_low_energy_nodes(Node **nodes, int num_nodes, double threshold, Node **low_energy_nodes, int *count) {
    *count = 0;
    for (int i = 0; i < num_nodes; i++) {
        if (calculate_energy_usage(nodes[i]) < threshold) {
            low_energy_nodes[*count] = nodes[i];
            (*count)++;
        }
    }
}

system/c_backend/memory/memory_management.c
C

#include <stdio.h>
#include <stdlib.h>
#include <string.h>
#include "memory_management.h"

// Function to allocate memory
void *allocate_memory(size_t size) {
    void *memory = malloc(size);
    if (memory == NULL) {
        fprintf(stderr, "Memory allocation failed\n");
        exit(EXIT_FAILURE);
    }
    return memory;
}

// Function to deallocate memory
void deallocate_memory(void *ptr) {
    free(ptr);
}

// Function to resize allocated memory
void *resize_memory(void *ptr, size_t new_size) {
    void *new_memory = realloc(ptr, new_size);
    if (new_memory == NULL) {
        fprintf(stderr, "Memory reallocation failed\n");
        exit(EXIT_FAILURE);
    }
    return new_memory;
}

// Function to copy memory
void copy_memory(void *dest, const void *src, size_t n) {
    memcpy(dest, src, n);
}

// Function to set memory
void set_memory(void *ptr, int value, size_t n) {
    memset(ptr, value, n);
}

system/c_backend/memory/pattern_operations.c
C

#include <stdio.h>
#include <stdlib.h>
#include <string.h>
#include "pattern_operations.h"
#include "memory_management.h"

// Function to allocate memory for a pattern
Pattern *create_pattern(int dimensions, int type) {
    Pattern *pattern = (Pattern *)allocate_memory(sizeof(Pattern));
    pattern->dimensions = dimensions;
    pattern->type = type;
    pattern->data = (double *)allocate_memory(dimensions * sizeof(double));
    return pattern;
}

// Function to free the memory allocated to a pattern
void free_pattern(Pattern *pattern) {
    deallocate_memory(pattern->data);
    deallocate_memory(pattern);
}

// Function to copy a pattern
Pattern *copy_pattern(const Pattern *src) {
    Pattern *dest = create_pattern(src->dimensions, src->type);
    copy_memory(dest->data, src->data, src->dimensions * sizeof(double));
    return dest;
}

// Function to compare two patterns
int compare_patterns(const Pattern *p1, const Pattern *p2) {
    if (p1->dimensions != p2->dimensions || p1->type != p2->type) {
        return 0; // Not equal
    }
    for (int i = 0; i < p1->dimensions; i++) {
        if (p1->data[i] != p2->data[i]) {
            return 0; // Not equal
        }
    }
    return 1; // Equal
}

// Function to serialize a pattern to a byte stream
// Note: This is a basic implementation and may need to be adapted based on the pattern structure
void serialize_pattern(const Pattern *pattern, char *buffer, int buffer_size) {
    int required_size = sizeof(pattern->dimensions) + sizeof(pattern->type) + pattern->dimensions * sizeof(double);
    if (buffer_size < required_size) {
        fprintf(stderr, "Buffer size is too small to serialize pattern\n");
        exit(EXIT_FAILURE);
    }

    char *ptr = buffer;
    memcpy(ptr, &(pattern->dimensions), sizeof(pattern->dimensions));
    ptr += sizeof(pattern->dimensions);
    memcpy(ptr, &(pattern->type), sizeof(pattern->type));
    ptr += sizeof(pattern->type);
    memcpy(ptr, pattern->data, pattern->dimensions * sizeof(double));
}

// Function to deserialize a pattern from a byte stream
Pattern *deserialize_pattern(const char *buffer) {
    const char *ptr = buffer;
    int dimensions, type;
    memcpy(&dimensions, ptr, sizeof(int));
    ptr += sizeof(int);
    memcpy(&type, ptr, sizeof(int));
    ptr += sizeof(int);

    Pattern *pattern = create_pattern(dimensions, type);
    memcpy(pattern->data, ptr, dimensions * sizeof(double));
    return pattern;
}

import unittest
import time
import numpy as np
from kaleidoscope.core.kaleidoscope_manager import KaleidoscopeManager
from kaleidoscope.node_management.core.node_lifecycle import NodeLifeCycleManager
from kaleidoscope.data_processing.realtime.stream_handler import StreamHandler
from kaleidoscope.data_processing.realtime.data_processor import DataProcessor
from kaleidoscope.data_processing.historical.data_storage import DataStorage
from kaleidoscope.data_processing.historical.data_analyzer import DataAnalyzer
from kaleidoscope.data_processing.historical.pattern_extractor import PatternExtractor
from kaleidoscope.data_processing.integration.data_pipeline import DataPipeline
from kaleidoscope.perspective_engine.core.advanced_resonance_processor import AdvancedResonanceProcessor
from kaleidoscope.perspective_engine.knowledge.hypothesis_generator import HypothesisGenerator
from kaleidoscope.perspective_engine.knowledge.graph_manager import GraphManager
from kaleidoscope.perspective_engine.core.pattern_analysis import PatternAnalyzer
from kaleidoscope.perspective_engine.simulation.scenario_generator import ScenarioGenerator
from kaleidoscope.perspective_engine.simulation.pattern_validator import PatternValidator

class TestSystemFlow(unittest.TestCase):

    def setUp(self):
        # Initialize components
        self.node_manager = NodeLifeCycleManager()
        self.data_storage = DataStorage(max_capacity=1000)
        self.data_analyzer = DataAnalyzer(self.data_storage)
        self.pattern_extractor = PatternExtractor(self.data_analyzer)
        self.data_pipeline = DataPipeline(None, self.data_storage, self.data_analyzer, self.pattern_extractor)
        self.kaleidoscope_manager = KaleidoscopeManager(node_manager=self.node_manager, data_pipeline=self.data_pipeline)
        self.data_processor = DataProcessor(self.kaleidoscope_manager.kaleidoscope_engine, self.kaleidoscope_manager.perspective_engine)
        self.stream_handler = StreamHandler(self.data_processor, batch_size=5, processing_interval=0.5)
        
        # Add the following lines to complete the setup
        self.graph_manager = GraphManager()
        self.hypothesis_generator = HypothesisGenerator(self.graph_manager)
        self.pattern_analyzer = PatternAnalyzer(self.kaleidoscope_manager.perspective_engine.advanced_resonance_processor.pattern_registry)
        self.scenario_generator = ScenarioGenerator(dimensions=self.kaleidoscope_manager.kaleidoscope_engine.dimensions)
        self.pattern_validator = PatternValidator(self.kaleidoscope_manager.perspective_engine.advanced_resonance_processor)
        
        # Register nodes
        self.node_manager.register_node(capacity=10.0, role="kaleidoscope_core", ip_address="192.168.1.1", port=8080)
        self.node_manager.register_node(capacity=8.0, role="perspective_engine", ip_address="192.168.1.2", port=8080)
        self.node_manager.register_node(capacity=5.0, role="data_processing", ip_address="192.168.1.3", port=8080)

    def test_stream_handler_to_data_processor(self):
        # Simulate data input
        for i in range(10):
            data = np.random.rand(self.kaleidoscope_manager.kaleidoscope_engine.dimensions, self.kaleidoscope_manager.kaleidoscope_engine.banks_per_dimension)
            self.stream_handler.add_data(data)

        # Start processing
        self.stream_handler.start_processing()
        time.sleep(2)  # Allow some time for processing
        self.stream_handler.stop_processing()

        # Verify data processing occurred
        # This will need to be adjusted based on your system's behavior
        self.assertTrue(len(self.kaleidoscope_manager.kaleidoscope_engine.resonance_manager.resonance_fields) > 0)

    def test_kaleidoscope_perspective_interaction(self):
        # Simulate data processing
        data = np.random.rand(self.kaleidoscope_manager.kaleidoscope_engine.dimensions, self.kaleidoscope_manager.kaleidoscope_engine.banks_per_dimension)
        self.data_processor.process_data(data)

        # Verify interaction
        # This will need to be adjusted based on your system's behavior
        self.assertTrue(len(self.kaleidoscope_manager.perspective_engine.advanced_resonance_processor.resonance_memory) > 0)

    def test_node_registration_and_task_assignment(self):
        # Register nodes
        node_id_1 = self.node_manager.register_node(capacity=10.0, role="test_role", ip_address="192.168.1.4", port=8080)
        node_id_2 = self.node_manager.register_node(capacity=5.0, role="test_role", ip_address="192.168.1.5", port=8080)

        # Create tasks
        tasks = [
            {"task_id": "task1", "required_role": "test_role", "required_capacity": 3.0},
            {"task_id": "task2", "required_role": "test_role", "required_capacity": 2.0}
        ]

        # Assign tasks
        for task in tasks:
            self.kaleidoscope_manager.task_distributor.distribute_tasks([task])

        # Verify task assignment
        self.assertTrue(len(self.node_manager.get_node(node_id_1).tasks) > 0)
        self.assertTrue(len(self.node_manager.get_node(node_id_2).tasks) > 0)

    def test_end_to_end_pattern_detection_and_analysis(self):
      # Simulate data input
      data_batch = [np.random.rand(self.kaleidoscope_manager.kaleidoscope_engine.dimensions, self.kaleidoscope_manager.kaleidoscope_engine.banks_per_dimension) for _ in range(10)]
      for data in data_batch:
          self.stream_handler.add_data(data)

      # Start processing
      self.stream_handler.start_processing()
      time.sleep(5)  # Allow some time for processing
      self.stream_handler.stop_processing()

      # Generate hypotheses
      patterns = []
      for node_id, node_data in self.kaleidoscope_manager.perspective_engine.advanced_resonance_processor.resonance_memory:
          patterns.extend(node_data)
      
      # Use the AdvancedResonanceProcessor to get advanced patterns
      advanced_patterns = self.kaleidoscope_manager.perspective_engine.advanced_resonance_processor.pattern_registry.get_frequent_patterns()
      patterns.extend(advanced_patterns)

      # Now pass these patterns to the hypothesis generator
      hypotheses = self.hypothesis_generator.generate_hypotheses(patterns)

      # Analyze patterns
      analysis_results = self.pattern_analyzer.analyze_patterns()

      # Verify pattern detection and analysis occurred
      self.assertTrue(len(patterns) > 0)
      self.assertTrue(len(hypotheses) > 0)
      self.assertTrue(len(analysis_results) > 0)
      
      # Generate and validate hypotheses
      hypotheses = self.hypothesis_generator.generate_hypotheses(patterns)
      validation_results = self.hypothesis_generator.validate_hypotheses(data_batch)

      # Generate scenarios based on current state and validate patterns
      initial_conditions = {'dim_0_value': 0.5, 'dim_1_value': -0.5}  # Example initial conditions
      scenarios = self.scenario_generator.generate_scenarios(initial_conditions, steps=10)
      for advanced_pattern in advanced_patterns:
        validation_result = self.pattern_validator._validate_pattern(advanced_pattern, scenarios)

        # Assertions to validate the results
        self.assertTrue(len(hypotheses) > 0)
        self.assertTrue(len(validation_results) > 0)
        self.assertTrue(len(scenarios) > 0)

    def tearDown(self):
      # Reset or clean up resources if necessary
      pass

if __name__ == '__main__':
    unittest.main()

import unittest
import time
import numpy as np
from kaleidoscope.data_processing.realtime.data_processor import DataProcessor
from kaleidoscope.data_processing.realtime.stream_handler import StreamHandler
from kaleidoscope.data_processing.historical.data_analyzer import DataAnalyzer
from kaleidoscope.data_processing.historical.pattern_extractor import PatternExtractor
from kaleidoscope.data_processing.integration.data_pipeline import DataPipeline
from kaleidoscope.core.kaleidoscope_manager import KaleidoscopeManager
from kaleidoscope.node_management.core.node_lifecycle import NodeLifeCycleManager

class TestDataProcessing(unittest.TestCase):

    def setUp(self):
        # Initialize the node manager
        self.node_manager = NodeLifeCycleManager()

        # Initialize the data pipeline components
        self.data_storage = MagicMock()  # Mocking DataStorage for simplicity
        self.data_analyzer = DataAnalyzer(self.data_storage)
        self.pattern_extractor = PatternExtractor(self.data_analyzer)
        self.data_pipeline = DataPipeline(None, self.data_storage, self.data_analyzer, self.pattern_extractor)

        # Initialize the Kaleidoscope manager with mocked node manager and data pipeline
        self.kaleidoscope_manager = KaleidoscopeManager(node_manager=self.node_manager, data_pipeline=self.data_pipeline)

        # Initialize the data processor with mocked engines
        self.data_processor = DataProcessor(self.kaleidoscope_manager.kaleidoscope_engine, self.kaleidoscope_manager.perspective_engine)

        # Initialize the stream handler
        self.stream_handler = StreamHandler(self.data_processor, batch_size=5, processing_interval=0.5)

        # Register nodes for testing

        self.node_manager.register_node(capacity=8.0, role="data_analysis", ip_address="192.168.1.7", port=8080)

    def test_data_processor_integration(self):
        # Simulate data input
        data = np.random.rand(self.kaleidoscope_manager.kaleidoscope_engine.dimensions, self.kaleidoscope_manager.kaleidoscope_engine.banks_per_dimension)
        
        # Process data
        result = self.data_processor.process_and_analyze(data)

        # Verify output structure
        self.assertIn('kaleidoscope', result)
        self.assertIn('perspective', result)
        self.assertIn('advanced_resonance', result)
        # self.assertIn('temporal_patterns', result)
        # self.assertIn('spatial_patterns', result)
        # self.assertIn('causal_patterns', result)
        # self.assertIn('advanced_patterns', result)

    def test_stream_handler_integration(self):
        # Add data to stream handler
        for _ in range(10):
            data = np.random.rand(self.kaleidoscope_manager.kaleidoscope_engine.dimensions, self.kaleidoscope_manager.kaleidoscope_engine.banks_per_dimension)
            self.stream_handler.add_data(data)

        # Start processing
        self.stream_handler.start_processing()
        time.sleep(2)  # Allow some time for processing
        self.stream_handler.stop_processing()

        # Assert that processing occurred (e.g., resonance fields updated)
        self.assertTrue(np.any(self.kaleidoscope_manager.kaleidoscope_engine.resonance_manager.resonance_fields != 0))

    def test_data_pipeline_integration(self):
        # Mock data storage behavior
        self.data_storage.retrieve_data.return_value = [
            {'timestamp': time.time() - 7200, 'data': [0.5, 0.6]},  # Example data point
            {'timestamp': time.time() - 3600, 'data': [0.7, 0.8]}   # Example data point
        ]

        # Trigger historical data analysis
        self.data_pipeline.analyze_historical_data()

        # Verify that historical analysis was performed
        self.data_storage.retrieve_data.assert_called()
        # self.assertTrue(len(self.pattern_extractor.extracted_patterns) > 0)
        
    def tearDown(self):
        # Clean up resources
        self.stream_handler.stop_processing()

if __name__ == '__main__':
    unittest.main()

system/tests/performance/test_scalability.py
Python

import unittest
import time
import numpy as np
from kaleidoscope.core.kaleidoscope_manager import KaleidoscopeManager
from kaleidoscope.node_management.core.node_lifecycle import NodeLifeCycleManager
from kaleidoscope.data_processing.realtime.data_processor import DataProcessor
from kaleidoscope.data_processing.realtime.stream_handler import StreamHandler
from kaleidoscope.data_processing.historical.data_storage import DataStorage
from kaleidoscope.data_processing.historical.data_analyzer import DataAnalyzer
from kaleidoscope.data_processing.historical.pattern_extractor import PatternExtractor
from kaleidoscope.data_processing.integration.data_pipeline import DataPipeline
class TestScalability(unittest.TestCase):

    def setUp(self):
        # Initialize a basic system setup
        self.node_manager = NodeLifeCycleManager()
        self.data_storage = DataStorage(max_capacity=5000)
        self.data_analyzer = DataAnalyzer(self.data_storage)
        self.pattern_extractor = PatternExtractor(self.data_analyzer)
        self.data_pipeline = DataPipeline(None, self.data_storage, self.data_analyzer, self.pattern_extractor)
        self.kaleidoscope_manager = KaleidoscopeManager(node_manager=self.node_manager, data_pipeline=self.data_pipeline)
        self.data_processor = DataProcessor(self.kaleidoscope_manager.kaleidoscope_engine, self.kaleidoscope_manager.perspective_engine)
        self.stream_handler = StreamHandler(self.data_processor, batch_size=10, processing_interval=1.0)

    def register_multiple_nodes(self, num_nodes, capacity, role):
        """Helper function to register multiple nodes."""
        for i in range(num_nodes):
            self.node_manager.register_node(capacity=capacity, role=role, ip_address=f"192.168.1.{i+1}", port=8080)

    def test_node_scalability(self):
        # Test scalability by increasing the number of nodes
        num_nodes_list = [10, 50, 100]
        for num_nodes in num_nodes_list:
            with self.subTest(num_nodes=num_nodes):
                self.node_manager = NodeLifeCycleManager()  # Reset node manager
                self.kaleidoscope_manager.node_manager = self.node_manager #update kaleidoscope manager to reflect new node manager
                self.register_multiple_nodes(num_nodes, 5.0, "data_processing")
                
                start_time = time.time()
                tasks = [{"task_id": f"task_{i}", "required_role": "data_processing", "required_capacity": 1.0} for i in range(num_nodes * 2)]
                self.kaleidoscope_manager.task_distributor.distribute_tasks(tasks)
                end_time = time.time()

                duration = end_time - start_time
                print(f"Time taken to distribute tasks to {num_nodes} nodes: {duration} seconds")
                self.assertLessEqual(duration, num_nodes * 0.1)  # Example threshold

    def test_data_processing_scalability(self):
        # Test scalability by increasing the data volume
        data_volumes = [100, 500, 1000]  # Example data volumes
        for volume in data_volumes:
            with self.subTest(data_volume=volume):
                self.stream_handler.stop_processing()  # Ensure stream handler is stopped before

-------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
Python

                # reinitializing it
                self.stream_handler = StreamHandler(self.data_processor, batch_size=10, processing_interval=2.0) # Reinitialize stream handler for each test case
                start_time = time.time()
                for _ in range(volume):
                    data = np.random.rand(self.kaleidoscope_manager.kaleidoscope_engine.dimensions, self.kaleidoscope_manager.kaleidoscope_engine.banks_per_dimension)
                    self.stream_handler.add_data(data)
                
                self.stream_handler.start_processing()
                time.sleep(5)  # Allow some time for processing
                self.stream_handler.stop_processing()
                end_time = time.time()

                duration = end_time - start_time
                print(f"Time taken to process {volume} data units: {duration} seconds")
                self.assertLessEqual(duration, volume * 0.05)  # Example threshold
                
    def tearDown(self):
        # Clean up resources
        self.stream_handler.stop_processing()

if __name__ == '__main__':
    unittest.main()

system/tests/performance/test_resource_usage.py
Python

import unittest
import time
import numpy as np
import psutil
from kaleidoscope.core.kaleidoscope_manager import KaleidoscopeManager
from kaleidoscope.node_management.core.node_lifecycle import NodeLifeCycleManager
from kaleidoscope.data_processing.realtime.data_processor import DataProcessor
from kaleidoscope.data_processing.realtime.stream_handler import StreamHandler
from kaleidoscope.data_processing.historical.data_storage import DataStorage
from kaleidoscope.data_processing.historical.data_analyzer import DataAnalyzer
from kaleidoscope.data_processing.historical.pattern_extractor import PatternExtractor
from kaleidoscope.data_processing.integration.data_pipeline import DataPipeline
class TestResourceUsage(unittest.TestCase):

    def setUp(self):
        # Initialize a basic system setup
        self.node_manager = NodeLifeCycleManager()
        self.data_storage = DataStorage()
        self.data_analyzer = DataAnalyzer(self.data_storage)
        self.pattern_extractor = PatternExtractor(self.data_analyzer)
        self.data_pipeline = DataPipeline(None, self.data_storage, self.data_analyzer, self.pattern_extractor)
        self.kaleidoscope_manager = KaleidoscopeManager(node_manager=self.node_manager, data_pipeline=self.data_pipeline)
        self.data_processor = DataProcessor(self.kaleidoscope_manager.kaleidoscope_engine, self.kaleidoscope_manager.perspective_engine)
        self.stream_handler = StreamHandler(self.data_processor, batch_size=10, processing_interval=1.0)

        # Register a few nodes
        self.node_manager.register_node(capacity=10.0, role="kaleidoscope_core", ip_address="192.168.1.10", port=8080)
        self.node_manager.register_node(capacity=8.0, role="perspective_engine", ip_address="192.168.1.11", port=8080)

    def test_cpu_usage(self):
        # Test CPU usage during data processing
        process = psutil.Process()
        start_cpu_usage = process.cpu_percent(interval=None)

        data = np.random.rand(self.kaleidoscope_manager.kaleidoscope_engine.dimensions, self.kaleidoscope_manager.kaleidoscope_engine.banks_per_dimension)
        self.data_processor.process_data(data)

        end_cpu_usage = process.cpu_percent(interval=None)
        print(f"CPU Usage: Start = {start_cpu_usage}%, End = {end_cpu_usage}%")
        self.assertLessEqual(end_cpu_usage - start_cpu_usage, 50)  # Example threshold

    def test_memory_usage(self):
        # Test memory usage during data processing
        process = psutil.Process()
        start_memory_usage = process.memory_info().rss

        data = np.random.rand(self.kaleidoscope_manager.kaleidoscope_engine.dimensions, self.kaleidoscope_manager.kaleidoscope_engine.banks_per_dimension)
        self.data_processor.process_data(data)

        end_memory_usage = process.memory_info().rss
        usage_increase = (end_memory_usage - start_memory_usage) / (1024 * 1024)  # in MB
        print(f"Memory Usage Increase: {usage_increase} MB")
        self.assertLessEqual(usage_increase, 100)  # Example threshold

    def test_network_usage(self):
        # Test network usage during data streaming
        net_io_before = psutil.net_io_counters()
        self.stream_handler.start_processing()
        time.sleep(5)  # Allow some time for network activity
        self.stream_handler.stop_processing()
        net_io_after = psutil.net_io_counters()

        sent_diff = net_io_after.bytes_sent - net_io_before.bytes_sent
        recv_diff = net_io_after.bytes_recv - net_io_before.bytes_recv
        print(f"Network Usage: Sent = {sent_diff} bytes, Received = {recv_diff} bytes")
        self.assertLessEqual(sent_diff, 100000)  # Example threshold
        self.assertLessEqual(recv_diff, 100000)
        
    def tearDown(self):
        # Clean up resources
        self.stream_handler.stop_processing()

if __name__ == '__main__':
    unittest.main()

import unittest
import time
import numpy as np
from unittest.mock import MagicMock
from kaleidoscope.data_processing.historical.data_analyzer import DataAnalyzer

class TestDataAnalyzer(unittest.TestCase):

    def setUp(self):
        self.mock_data_storage = MagicMock()
        self.analyzer = DataAnalyzer(self.mock_data_storage)

    def test_initialization(self):
        self.assertEqual(self.analyzer.data_storage, self.mock_data_storage)

    def test_analyze_historical_data(self):
        # Mock data retrieval
        self.mock_data_storage.retrieve_data.return_value = [
            {'data': [1.0, 2.0]},
            {'data': [2.0, 3.0]},
            {'data': [3.0, 4.0]}
        ]

        analysis = self.analyzer.analyze_historical_data(0, 1)
        self.assertIn('mean', analysis)
        self.assertIn('std', analysis)
        self.assertIn('min', analysis)
        self.assertIn('max', analysis)
        self.assertIn('correlations', analysis)

    def test_calculate_correlations(self):
        data_array = np.array([[1.0, 2.0], [2.0, 3.0], [3.0, 4.0]])
        correlations = self.analyzer._calculate_correlations(data_array)
        self.assertIn('dim_0_vs_dim_1', correlations)
        self.assertAlmostEqual(correlations['dim_0_vs_dim_1'], 1.0)  # Perfect correlation

    def test_get_state(self):
        self.mock_data_storage.data = [
            {'timestamp': time.time() - 7200, 'data': [0.5, 0.6]},
            {'timestamp': time.time() - 3600, 'data': [0.7, 0.8]}
        ]
        self.mock_data_storage.retrieve_data.return_value = self.mock_data_storage.data
        state = self.analyzer.get_state()
        self.assertIn('data_storage_size', state)
        self.assertIn('last_analysis', state)
        self.assertTrue(len(state['last_analysis']) > 0)

if __name__ == '__main__':
    unittest.main()

import unittest
from unittest.mock import MagicMock
from kaleidoscope.data_processing.historical.pattern_extractor import PatternExtractor

class TestPatternExtractor(unittest.TestCase):

    def setUp(self):
        self.mock_data_analyzer = MagicMock()
        self.extractor = PatternExtractor(self.mock_data_analyzer)

    def test_initialization(self):
        self.assertEqual(self.extractor.data_analyzer, self.mock_data_analyzer)
        self.assertEqual(len(self.extractor.extracted_patterns), 0)

    def test_extract_patterns(self):
        self.mock_data_analyzer.analyze_historical_data.return_value = {
            'start_time': 0,
            'end_time': 1,
            'mean': [0.5, 0.6],
            'std': [0.1, 0.1],
            'min': [0.4, 0.5],
            'max': [0.6, 0.7],
            'correlations': {}
        }
        self.mock_data_analyzer.data_storage.retrieve_data.return_value = [
            {'timestamp': 0, 'data': [0.5, 0.6]},
            {'timestamp': 1, 'data': [0.6, 0.7]}
        ]

        # Test trend extraction
        trends = self.extractor.extract_patterns(0, 1, 'trend')
        self.assertTrue(len(trends) > 0)
        self.assertEqual(trends[0]['type'], 'trend')

        # Test anomaly extraction
        anomalies = self.extractor.extract_patterns(0, 1, 'anomaly')
        self.assertTrue(len(anomalies) > 0)
        self.assertEqual(anomalies[0]['type'], 'anomaly')

    def test_extract_trends(self):
        analysis_results = {
            'start_time': 0,
            'end_time': 1,
            'mean': [0.5, 0.6, 0.4],
            'std': [0.1, 0.1, 0.1],
            'min': [0.4, 0.5, 0.3],
            'max': [0.6, 0.7, 0.5],
            'correlations': {}
        }
        trends = self.extractor._extract_trends(analysis_results)
        self.assertTrue(len(trends) > 0)
        for trend in trends:
            self.assertEqual(trend['type'], 'trend')
            self.assertIn('pattern', trend)
            self.assertIn('dimension', trend)

    def test_extract_anomalies(self):
        analysis_results = {
            'start_time': 0,
            'end_time': 1,
            'mean': [0.5, 0.6],
            'std': [0.1, 0.1],
            'min': [0.4, 0.5],
            'max': [0.6, 0.7],
            'correlations': {}
        }
        self.mock_data_analyzer.data_storage.retrieve_data.return_value = [
            {'timestamp': 0, 'data': [0.5, 0.6]},
            {'timestamp': 1, 'data': [0.9, 0.8]}  # Anomalies
        ]
        anomalies = self.extractor._extract_anomalies(analysis_results)
        self.assertTrue(len(anomalies) > 0)
        for anomaly in anomalies:
            self.assertEqual(anomaly['type'], 'anomaly')
            self.assertIn('dimension', anomaly)
            self.assertIn('value', anomaly)
            self.assertIn('z_score', anomaly)

    def test_get_state(self):
        state = self.extractor.get_state()
        self.assertIn('extracted_patterns_count', state)
        self.assertIn('last_extraction', state)

if __name__ == '__main__':
    unittest.main()

import unittest
from unittest.mock import MagicMock
from kaleidoscope.data_processing.integration.data_pipeline import DataPipeline

class TestDataPipeline(unittest.TestCase):

    def setUp(self):
        self.mock_stream_handler = MagicMock()
        self.mock_data_storage = MagicMock()
        self.mock_historical_analyzer = MagicMock()
        self.mock_pattern_extractor = MagicMock()
        self.pipeline = DataPipeline(self.mock_stream_handler, self.mock_data_storage, self.mock_historical_analyzer, self.mock_pattern_extractor)

    def test_initialization(self):
        self.assertEqual(self.pipeline.stream_handler, self.mock_stream_handler)
        self.assertEqual(self.pipeline.data_storage, self.mock_data_storage)
        self.assertEqual(self.pipeline.historical_analyzer, self.mock_historical_analyzer)
        self.assertEqual(self.pipeline.pattern_extractor, self.mock_pattern_extractor)

    def test_run_pipeline(self):
        self.pipeline.run_pipeline()
        self.mock_stream_handler.start_processing.assert_called_once()

    def test_analyze_historical_data(self):
        self.mock_historical_analyzer.analyze_historical_data.return_value = {'result': 'analysis'}
        self.mock_pattern_extractor.extract_patterns.return_value = ['pattern1', 'pattern2']
        self.pipeline.analyze_historical_data()
        self.mock_historical_analyzer.analyze_historical_data.assert_called()
        self.mock_pattern_extractor.extract_patterns.assert_called()

    def test_get_state(self):
        self.mock_stream_handler.get_state.return_value = {'stream_handler': 'state'}
        self.mock_data_storage.get_state.return_value = {'data_storage': 'state'}
        self.mock_historical_analyzer.get_state.return_value = {'historical_analyzer': 'state'}
        self.mock_pattern_extractor.get_state.return_value = {'pattern_extractor': 'state'}
        state = self.pipeline.get_state()
        self.assertIn('stream_handler_state', state)
        self.assertIn('data_storage_state', state)
        self.assertIn('historical_analyzer_state', state)
        self.assertIn('pattern_extractor_state', state)

if __name__ == '__main__':
    unittest.main()

import unittest
import numpy as np
from kaleidoscope.data_processing.integration.transformation_engine import TransformationEngine

class TestTransformationEngine(unittest.TestCase):

    def setUp(self):
        self.dimensions = 3
        self.engine = TransformationEngine(self.dimensions)

    def test_initialization(self):
        self.assertEqual(self.engine.dimensions, self.dimensions)
        self.assertEqual(len(self.engine.transformation_matrices), self.dimensions)
        for dim in range(self.dimensions):
            self.assertTrue(np.array_equal(self.engine.transformation_matrices[dim], np.eye(dim + 1)))

    def test_transform_data(self):
        data = np.array([1, 2, 3])
        dim = 2
        transformed_data = self.engine.transform_data(data, dim)
        self.assertTrue(np.array_equal(transformed_data, data))  # Identity matrix transformation

    def test_update_transformation_matrix(self):
        new_matrix = np.array([[0, 1], [1, 0]])
        dim = 1
        self.engine.update_transformation_matrix(dim, new_matrix)
        self.assertTrue(np.array_equal(self.engine.transformation_matrices[dim], new_matrix))

    def test_get_transformation_matrix(self):
        dim = 1
        matrix = self.engine.get_transformation_matrix(dim)
        self.assertTrue(np.array_equal(matrix, np.eye(dim + 1)))

    def test_transform_data_invalid_dimension(self):
        with self.assertRaises(ValueError):
            self.engine.transform_data(np.array([1, 2]), self.dimensions + 1)

    def test_update_transformation_matrix_invalid_shape(self):
        with self.assertRaises(ValueError):
            self.engine.update_transformation_matrix(0, np.array([[1, 2]]))

    def test_get_state(self):
        state = self.engine.get_state()
        self.

------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

        self.assertEqual(state['dimensions'], self.dimensions)
        self.assertEqual(len(state['transformation_matrices']), self.dimensions)
        for dim in range(self.dimensions):

system/tests/unit/test_quantum_math.py
Python

import unittest
import numpy as np
from kaleidoscope.utils.quantum_math import QuantumMath

class TestQuantumMath(unittest.TestCase):

    def test_create_entangled_state(self):
        num_dimensions = 2
        state = QuantumMath.create_entangled_state(num_dimensions)
        self.assertEqual(state.shape, (2**num_dimensions,))
        self.assertAlmostEqual(np.linalg.norm(state), 1.0)

    def test_apply_hadamard(self):
        state = np.array([1, 0, 0, 0])  # Example state for 2 dimensions
        transformed_state = QuantumMath.apply_hadamard(state)
        self.assertEqual(transformed_state.shape, (4,))
        self.assertAlmostEqual(np.linalg.norm(transformed_state), 1.0)

    def test_measure_state(self):
        state = np.array([1/np.sqrt(2), 1/np.sqrt(2)])
        measurement = QuantumMath.measure_state(state)
        self.assertIn(measurement, [0, 1])

    def test_calculate_density_matrix(self):
        state = np.array([1/np.sqrt(2), 1/np.sqrt(2)])
        density_matrix = QuantumMath.calculate_density_matrix(state)
        self.assertEqual(density_matrix.shape, (2, 2))
        self.assertAlmostEqual(np.trace(density_matrix), 1.0)

    def test_calculate_purity(self):
        pure_state = np.array([1, 0])
        mixed_state = np.array([1/np.sqrt(2), 1/np.sqrt(2)])
        self.assertAlmostEqual(QuantumMath.calculate_purity(pure_state), 1.0)
        self.assertLess(QuantumMath.calculate_purity(mixed_state), 1.0)

    def test_calculate_entanglement_entropy(self):
        entangled_state = QuantumMath.create_entangled_state(2)
        non_entangled_state = np.array([1, 0, 0, 0])
        self.assertGreater(QuantumMath.calculate_entanglement_entropy(entangled_state), 0.0)
        self.assertEqual(QuantumMath.calculate_entanglement_entropy(non_entangled_state), 0.0)
    
    def test_unitary_transform(self):
        state = np.array([1, 0])
        hadamard = (1 / np.sqrt(2)) * np.array([[1, 1], [1, -1]])
        transformed_state = QuantumMath.unitary_transform(state, hadamard)
        self.assertAlmostEqual(np.linalg.norm(transformed_state), 1.0)

    def test_is_unitary(self):
        hadamard = (1 / np.sqrt(2)) * np.array([[1, 1], [1, -1]])
        non_unitary_matrix = np.array([[1, 2], [3, 4]])
        self.assertTrue(QuantumMath.is_unitary(hadamard))
        self.assertFalse(QuantumMath.is_unitary(non_unitary_matrix))

if __name__ == '__main__':
    unittest.main()

system/tests/unit/test_pattern_analysis.py

import unittest
from unittest.mock import MagicMock
import numpy as np
from kaleidoscope.utils.pattern_analysis import PatternAnalysis

class TestPatternAnalysis(unittest.TestCase):

    def setUp(self):
        self.analyzer = PatternAnalysis()

    def test_calculate_pattern_frequency(self):
        patterns = [
            {'type': 'pattern1'},
            {'type': 'pattern2'},
            {'type': 'pattern1'},
            {'type': 'pattern3'}
        ]
        frequency = self.analyzer.calculate_pattern_frequency(patterns)
        self.assertEqual(frequency['pattern1'], 2)
        self.assertEqual(frequency['pattern2'], 1)
        self.assertEqual(frequency['pattern3'], 1)

    def test_calculate_average_pattern_strength(self):
        patterns = [
            {'strength': 0.8},
            {'strength': 0.9},
            {'strength': 0.7}
        ]
        avg_strength = self.analyzer.calculate_average_pattern_strength(patterns)
        self.assertAlmostEqual(avg_strength, 0.8)

    def test_find_most_common_dimension(self):
        patterns = [
            {'dimensions': [0, 1]},
            {'dimensions': [1, 2]},
            {'dimensions': [1, 3]},
            {'dimension': 0}
        ]
        most_common = self.analyzer.find_most_common_dimension(patterns)
        self.assertEqual(most_common, 1)

    def test_calculate_pattern_correlations(self):
        patterns = [
            {'strength': 0.8, 'coherence': 0.9, 'dimension': 0},
            {'strength': 0.6, 'coherence': 0.7, 'dimension': 1},
            {'strength': 0.9, 'coherence': 0.8, 'dimension': 2}
        ]
        correlations = self.analyzer.calculate_pattern_correlations(patterns)
        self.assertIn('strength_vs_coherence', correlations)
        self.assertIn('coherence_vs_strength', correlations)
        self.assertTrue(isinstance(correlations['strength_vs_coherence'], float))

    def test_detect_anomalies(self):
        patterns = [
            {'strength': 0.8, 'coherence': 0.9},
            {'strength': 0.7, 'coherence': 0.8},
            {'strength': 0.85, 'coherence': 0.95},
            {'strength': 0.1, 'coherence': 0.2}  # Anomaly
        ]
        anomalies = self.analyzer.detect_anomalies(patterns, threshold=2.0)
        self.assertEqual(len(anomalies), 1)
        self.assertEqual(anomalies[0]['pattern']['strength'], 0.1)

    def test_analyze_pattern_evolution(self):
        # Mock AdvancedPatternRegistry
        mock_registry = MagicMock()
        mock_registry.analyze_pattern_evolution.return_value = [
            {
                'pattern_


_id': 'pattern1',
                'strength_changes': [(0, 0.8), (1, 0.85), (2, 0.9)],
                'coherence_changes': [(0, 0.7), (1, 0.75), (2, 0.8)],
            }
        ]
        
        evolution_analysis = PatternAnalysis.analyze_pattern_evolution(mock_registry)
        self.assertIn('pattern1', evolution_analysis)
        self.assertEqual(len(evolution_analysis['pattern1']), 2)
        self.assertIn('avg_strength_change', evolution_analysis['pattern1'])
        self.assertIn('avg_coherence_change', evolution_analysis['pattern1'])

if __name__ == '__main__':
    unittest.main()

system/tests/unit/test_system_helpers.py
Python

import unittest
import numpy as np
from kaleidoscope.utils.system_helpers import SystemHelpers

class TestSystemHelpers(unittest.TestCase):

    def test_normalize_data(self):
        data = np.array([1, 2, 3, 4, 5])
        normalized_data = SystemHelpers.normalize_data(data)
        self.assertTrue(np.all(normalized_data >= 0.0))
        self.assertTrue(np.all(normalized_data <= 1.0))
        self.assertAlmostEqual(np.min(normalized_data), 0.0)
        self.assertAlmostEqual(np.max(normalized_data), 1.0)

    def test_scale_data(self):
        data = np.array([1, 2, 3, 4, 5])
        scaled_data = SystemHelpers.scale_data(data, -1, 1)
        self.assertAlmostEqual(np.min(scaled_data), -1.0)
        self.assertAlmostEqual(np.max(scaled_data), 1.0)

    def test_apply_fft(self):
        data = np.array([1, 2, 3, 4])
        fft_data = SystemHelpers.apply_fft(data)
        self.assertEqual(fft_data.shape, data.shape)
        self.assertTrue(np.all(fft_data.dtype == np.complex128))

    def test_apply_inverse_fft(self):
        data = np.array([1 + 1j, 2 - 1j, 3 + 0j, 4 - 2j])
        ifft_data = SystemHelpers.apply_inverse_fft(data)
        self.assertEqual(ifft_data.shape, data.shape)
        self.assertTrue(np.all(ifft_data.dtype == np.complex128))

    def test_calculate_cross_correlation(self):
        data1 = np.array([1, 2, 3])
        data2 = np.array([3, 2, 1])
        correlation = SystemHelpers.calculate_cross_correlation(data1, data2)
        self.assertEqual(correlation.shape, (5,))  # 2 * len(data1) - 1

    def test_detect_outliers(self):
        data = np.array([1, 2, 3, 4, 10, 2, 3, 4, 2])  # 10 is an outlier
        outliers = SystemHelpers.detect_outliers(data, threshold=2.0)
        self.assertIn(4, outliers)  # Index 4 corresponds to the outlier value 10

if __name__ == '__main__':
    unittest.main()

system/tests/unit/test_data_storage.py
Python

import unittest
import os
from kaleidoscope.data_processing.historical.data_storage import DataStorage

class TestDataStorage(unittest.TestCase):

    def setUp(self):
        self.storage_directory = "test_storage"
        self.storage = DataStorage(max_capacity=3, storage_directory=self.storage_directory)

    def tearDown(self):
        # Clean up the test storage directory
        if os.path.exists(self.storage_directory):
            for file in os.listdir(self.storage_directory):
                os.remove(os.path.join(self.storage_directory, file))
            os.rmdir(self.storage_directory)

    def test_initialization(self):
        self.assertEqual(self.storage.max_capacity, 3)
        self.assertEqual(len(self.storage.data), 0)
        self.assertTrue(os.path.exists(self.storage_directory))

    def test_store_data(self):
        data_point = {'key': 'value'}
        self.storage.store_data(data_point)
        self.assertEqual(len(self.storage.data), 1)
        self.assertIn('timestamp', self.storage.data[0])
        self.assertEqual(self.storage.data[0]['data'], data_point)

    def test_retrieve_data(self):
        data_point1 = {'key': 'value1'}
        data_point2 = {'key': 'value2'}
        self.storage.store_data(data_point1)
        time.sleep(0.001)  # Ensure timestamps are different
        self.storage.store_data(data_point2)
        start_time = self.storage.data[0]['timestamp']
        end_time = self.storage.data[1]['timestamp']
        retrieved_data = self.storage.retrieve_data(start_time, end_time)
        self.assertEqual(len(retrieved_data), 2)
        self.assertEqual(retrieved_data[0], data_point1)
        self.assertEqual(retrieved_data[1], data_point2)

    def test_store_data_exceed_capacity(self):
        for i in range(4):
            self.storage.store_data({'key': f'value{i}'})
        self.assertEqual(len(self.storage.data), 3)
        self.assertEqual(self.storage.data[0]['data']['key'], 'value1')
        self.assertEqual(self.storage.data[2]['data']['key'], 'value3')

    def test_load_data_from_file(self):
        # Create a sample data file
        with open(os.path.join(self.storage_directory, 'data.json'), 'w') as f:
            f.write('{"timestamp": 1672531200, "data": {"key": "value1"}}\n')
            f.write('{"timestamp": 1672617600, "data": {"key": "value2"}}\n')

        self.storage.load_data_from_file()
        self.assertEqual(len(self.storage.data), 2)
        self.assertEqual(self.storage.data[0]['data']['key'], 'value1')
        self.assertEqual(self.storage.data[1]['data']['key'], 'value2')

    def test_clear_data(self):
        self.storage.store_data({'key': 'value1'})
        self.storage.clear_data()
        self.assertEqual(len(self.storage.data), 0)
        # Verify that the data file is empty
        with open(os.path.join(self.storage_directory, 'data.json'), 'r') as f:
            content = f.read()
        self.assertEqual(content, '')

    def test_get_state(self):
        self.storage.store_data({'key': 'value1'})
        state = self.storage.get_state()
        self.assertEqual(state['max_capacity'], 3)
        self.assertEqual(state['current_size'], 1)
        self.assertEqual(state['storage_directory'], self.storage_directory)
        
if __name__ == '__main__':
    unittest.main()

Modified kaleidoscope/core/quantum_engine.py:

Python

import numpy as np
import scipy.linalg as spl
import networkx as nx
import ctypes

class QuantumEngine:
    def __init__(self, dimensions: int = 12, banks_per_dimension: int = 3):
        self.dimensions = dimensions
        self.banks_per_dimension = banks_per_dimension
        self.quantum_states = np.zeros((dimensions, banks_per_dimension), dtype=np.complex128)
        self.entanglement_graph = nx.Graph()
        self.resonance_matrix = np.zeros((dimensions, dimensions), dtype=np.complex128)
        self._initialize_quantum_states()

        # Load the shared library
        self.c_lib = ctypes.CDLL("./c_backend/kaleidoscope_core.so")  # Adjust path if necessary

        # Define argument and return types for complex_fft
        self.c_lib.complex_fft.argtypes = [np.ctypeslib.ndpointer(np.complex128, flags="C_CONTIGUOUS"), ctypes.c_int]
        self.c_lib.complex_fft.restype = None

    def _initialize_quantum_states(self):
        """Initialize quantum states with superposition and entanglement."""
        # ... (rest of the initialization code remains the same)

    def process_data(self, data: np.ndarray) -> np.ndarray:
        """Process input data through quantum transformations and resonance."""
        transformed_data = self._apply_quantum_transformations(data)
        resonance_effects = self._calculate_resonance(transformed_data)
        return resonance_effects

    def _apply_quantum_transformations(self, data: np.ndarray) -> np.ndarray:
        """Apply quantum transformations to input data using matrix operations."""
        transformed_data = np.zeros_like(data, dtype=np.complex128)
        for dim in range(self.dimensions):
            for bank in range(self.banks_per_dimension):
                # Apply Hadamard gate for superposition
                hadamard_transformed = self._hadamard_transform(self.quantum_states[dim, bank])

                # Apply phase rotation based on entanglement
                phase_transformed = self._apply_phase_rotation(hadamard_transformed, dim)

                # Update quantum state
                self.quantum_states[dim, bank] = phase_transformed

                # Map input data to quantum state
                transformed_data[dim] += phase_transformed * data[dim, bank % data.shape[1]]

        # Apply FFT using the C backend
        for i in range(transformed_data.shape[0]):
            self.c_lib.complex_fft(transformed_data[i], transformed_data.shape[1])

        return transformed_data

    # ... (rest of the QuantumEngine class remains the same)

    def add_complex_arrays(self, arr1, arr2):
        if arr1.shape != arr2.shape:
            raise ValueError("Arrays must have the same shape for element-wise addition.")
        result = np.zeros_like(arr1)
        self.c_lib.complex_array_add(arr1, arr2, result, arr1.size)
        return result

    # Add a method to use complex_array_multiply
    def multiply_complex_arrays(self, arr1, arr2):
        if arr1.shape != arr2.shape:
            raise ValueError("Arrays must have the same shape for element-wise multiplication.")
        result = np.zeros_like(arr1)
        self.c_lib.complex_array_multiply(arr1, arr2, result, arr1.size)

2. Integrate initialize_bank, store_data, and retrieve_data:

We'll integrate these functions into the MemoryBank class in Python.

Modified kaleidoscope/memory/banks.py:
Python

import numpy as np
import ctypes

class MemoryBank:
    def __init__(self, capacity: int, dimensions: int):
        self.capacity = capacity
        self.dimensions = dimensions

        # Load the shared library
        self.c_lib = ctypes.CDLL("./c_backend/kaleidoscope_core.so")  # Adjust the path if necessary

        # Define argument types and return types for the C functions
        self.c_lib.initialize_bank.argtypes = [ctypes.POINTER(ctypes.c_void_p), ctypes.c_int, ctypes.c_int]
        self.c_lib.initialize_bank.restype = None

        self.c_lib.store_data.argtypes = [ctypes.POINTER(ctypes.c_void_p), np.ctypeslib.ndpointer(np.complex128, flags="C_CONTIGUOUS")]
        self.c_lib.store_data.restype = None

        self.c_lib.retrieve_data.argtypes = [ctypes.POINTER(ctypes.c_void_p), ctypes.c_int, np.ctypeslib.ndpointer(np.complex128, flags="C_CONTIGUOUS")]
        self.c_lib.retrieve_data.restype = None

        self.c_lib.get_recent_data.argtypes = [ctypes.POINTER(ctypes.c_void_p), ctypes.c_int, np.ctypeslib.ndpointer(np.complex128, flags="C_CONTIGUOUS")]
        self.c_lib.get_recent_data.restype = None

        self.c_lib.free_bank.argtypes = [ctypes.POINTER(ctypes.c_void_p)]
        self.c_lib.free_bank.restype = None

        # Initialize the bank using the C function
        self.bank_ptr = ctypes.c_void_p()
        self.c_lib.initialize_bank(ctypes.byref(self.bank_ptr), ctypes.c_int(self.capacity), ctypes.c_int(self.dimensions))

    def store(self, data_vector: np.ndarray):
        """Store a data vector in the memory bank."""
        if data_vector.shape[0] != self.dimensions:
            raise ValueError("Data vector dimensions do not match memory bank dimensions.")

        # Store data using the C function
        self.c_lib.store_data(self.bank_ptr, data_vector)

    def retrieve(self, index: int) -> np.ndarray:
        """Retrieve a data vector from a specific index in the memory bank."""
        if index < 0 or index >= self.capacity:
            raise IndexError("Index out of range.")

        retrieved_vector = np.zeros(self.dimensions, dtype=np.complex128)
        # Retrieve data using the C function
        self.c_lib.retrieve_data(self.bank_ptr, ctypes.c_int(index), retrieved_vector)
        return retrieved_vector

    def get_recent(self, n: int) -> np.ndarray:
        """Retrieve the n most recently stored data vectors."""
        if n > self.capacity:
            raise ValueError("Cannot retrieve more elements than the capacity of the memory bank.")

        recent_data = np.zeros((n, self.dimensions), dtype=np.complex128)
        # Get recent data using the C function
        self.c_lib.get_recent_data(self.bank_ptr, ctypes.c_int(n), recent_data)
        return recent_data

    def __del__(self):
        """Free the memory allocated to the bank when the object is deleted."""
        self.c_lib.free_bank(self.bank_ptr)

    # ... (rest of the MemoryBank class)

Choosing the Best Approach:

    For initial testing and debugging, print statements/logging are the easiest to implement.
    For more robust testing, using return values is generally the best approach. You can define specific error codes or return values that indicate success or different types of failures.
    Modifying data in C and then checking it in Python can be useful but ensure the modifications are predictable and well-documented.

1. Modify C Functions to Return Status Codes:

We'll modify the following C functions to return an integer status code:

    complex_fft
    complex_array_add
    complex_array_multiply
    initialize_bank
    store_data
    retrieve_data
    get_recent_data
    hadamard_transform
    phase_rotation

We'll use the following convention:

    0: Success
    Non-zero: Error (you can define specific error codes for different types of errors)

system/c_backend/core/core_processing.c:
C

#include <stdio.h>
#include <stdlib.h>
#include <math.h>
#include <complex.h>
#include "core_processing.h"

// ... (other functions)

// Function to perform complex Fast Fourier Transform (FFT)
int complex_fft(double complex *data, int n) {
    printf("complex_fft called with n = %d\n", n); // Keep this for now to track execution
    if (n <= 1) return 0; // Base case: nothing to do

    // Allocate memory for even and odd parts
    double complex *even = (double complex *)malloc((n / 2) * sizeof(double complex));
    double complex *odd = (double complex *)malloc((n / 2) * sizeof(double complex));
    if (even == NULL || odd == NULL) {
        fprintf(stderr, "Memory allocation failed in complex_fft\n");
        return 1; // Indicate memory allocation failure
    }

    // Divide the array into even and odd parts
    for (int i = 0; i < n / 2; i++) {
        even[i] = data[2 * i];
        odd[i] = data[2 * i + 1];
    }

    // Recursive calls for even and odd parts
    int ret_even = complex_fft(even, n / 2);
    int ret_odd = complex_fft(odd, n / 2);

    // Check for errors in recursive calls
    if (ret_even != 0 || ret_odd != 0) {
        free(even);
        free(odd);
        return 2; // Indicate error in recursive calls
    }

    // Combine the results
    for (int k = 0; k < n / 2; k++) {
        double complex t = cexp(-2 * M_PI * I * k / n) * odd[k];
        data[k] = even[k] + t;
        data[k + n / 2] = even[k] - t;
    }

    // Free allocated memory
    free(even);
    free(odd);

    // Modify the first element for testing purposes
    data[0] = 100.0 + 100.0 * I;

    return 0; // Indicate success
}

// Function to perform element-wise addition of two complex arrays
int complex_array_add(double complex *arr1, double complex *arr2, double complex *result, int n) {
    for (int i = 0; i < n; i++) {
        result[i] = arr1[i] + arr2[i];
    }
    return 0; // Indicate success
}

// Function to perform element-wise multiplication of two complex arrays
int complex_array_multiply(double complex *arr1, double complex *arr2, double complex *result, int n) {
    for (int i = 0; i < n; i++) {
        result[i] = arr1[i] * arr2[i];
    }
    return 0; // Indicate success
}

system/c_backend/core/bank_operations.c:
C

#include <stdio.h>
#include <stdlib.h>
#include <string.h>
#include "bank_operations.h"

// Function to initialize a memory bank
int initialize_bank(MemoryBank *bank, int capacity, int dimensions) {
    bank->capacity = capacity;
    bank->dimensions = dimensions;
    bank->data = (double complex *)malloc(capacity * dimensions * sizeof(double complex));
    bank->head = 0;
    if (bank->data == NULL) {
        fprintf(stderr, "Memory allocation failed for bank data\n");
        return 1; // Indicate memory allocation failure
    }
    return 0; // Indicate success
}

// Function to store a data vector in the memory bank
int store_data(MemoryBank *bank, double complex *data_vector) {
    int index = (bank->head * bank->dimensions);
    memcpy(&bank->data[index], data_vector, bank->dimensions * sizeof(double complex));
    bank->head = (bank->head + 1) % bank->capacity;
    return 0; // Indicate success
}

// Function to retrieve a data vector from the memory bank
int retrieve_data(MemoryBank *bank, int index, double complex *data_vector) {
    if (index < 0 || index >= bank->capacity) {
        fprintf(stderr, "Index out of range for memory bank retrieval\n");
        return 2; // Indicate out-of-range error
    }
    int data_index = (index * bank->dimensions);
    memcpy(data_vector, &bank->data[data_index], bank->dimensions * sizeof(double complex));
    return 0; // Indicate success
}

// Function to get the n most recently stored data vectors
int get_recent_data(MemoryBank *bank, int n, double complex *recent_data) {
    if (n > bank->capacity) {
        fprintf(stderr, "Cannot retrieve more elements than the capacity of the memory bank\n");
        return 2; // Indicate invalid argument
    }
    int start = (bank->head - n + bank->capacity) % bank->capacity;
    for (int i = 0; i < n; i++) {
        int index = ((start + i) % bank->capacity) * bank->dimensions;
        memcpy(&recent_data[i * bank->dimensions], &bank->data[index], bank->dimensions * sizeof(double complex));
    }
    return 0; // Indicate success
}

// ... (other functions remain the same)

system/c_backend/core/quantum_operations.c:
C

#include <stdio.h>
#include <stdlib.h>
#include <math.h>
#include <complex.h>
#include "quantum_operations.h"

// Function to apply the Hadamard transformation to a quantum state
int hadamard_transform(double complex *state) {
    double complex temp = *state;
    *state = (temp + (1 - temp)) / sqrt(2);
    return 0;  // Indicate success
}

// Function to apply a phase rotation to a quantum state
int phase_rotation(double complex *state, double phase) {
    *state *= cexp(I * phase);
    return 0; // Indicate success
}

            np.ctypeslib.ndpointer(np.complex128, flags="C_CONTIGUOUS"),
            np.ctypeslib.ndpointer(np.complex128, flags="C_CONTIGUOUS"),
            ctypes.c_int
        ]
        self.c_lib.complex_array_add.restype = ctypes.c_int # Return type is int

        self.c_lib.complex_array_multiply.argtypes = [
            np.ctypeslib.ndpointer(np.complex128, flags="C_CONTIGUOUS"),
            np.ctypeslib.ndpointer(np.complex128, flags="C_CONTIGUOUS"),
            np.ctypeslib.ndpointer(np.complex128, flags="C_CONTIGUOUS"),
            ctypes.c_int
        ]
        self.c_lib.complex_array_multiply.restype = ctypes.c_int # Return type is int

        # Define argument and return types for hadamard_transform
        self.c_lib.hadamard_transform.argtypes = [np.ctypeslib.ndpointer(np.complex128, flags="C_CONTIGUOUS")]
        self.c_lib.hadamard_transform.restype = ctypes.c_int # Return type is int

        # Define argument and return types for phase_rotation
        self.c_lib.phase_rotation.argtypes = [np.ctypeslib.ndpointer(np.complex128, flags="C_CONTIGUOUS"), ctypes.c_double]
        self.c_lib.phase_rotation.restype = ctypes.c_int # Return type is int

    # ...
    def _apply_quantum_transformations(self, data: np.ndarray) -> np.ndarray:
        # ... (previous code)

        # Apply FFT using the C backend
        for i in range(transformed_data.shape[0]):
            result = self.c_lib.complex_fft(transformed_data[i], transformed_data.shape[1])
            if result != 0:
                raise RuntimeError(f"Error in complex_fft for dimension {i}, error code: {result}")

        return transformed_data
        
    def _hadamard_transform(self, state: complex) -> complex:
        """Apply Hadamard transformation to a given quantum state."""
        state_np = np.array([state], dtype=np.complex128)
        result = self.c_lib.hadamard_transform(state_np)
        if result != 0:
            raise RuntimeError(f"Error in hadamard_transform, error code: {result}")
        return state_np[0]

    def _apply_phase_rotation(self, state: complex, dim: int) -> complex:
        """Apply phase rotation based on entanglement with other dimensions."""
        phase_shift = 0
        for other_dim in self.entanglement_graph.neighbors(dim):
            entanglement_strength = np.abs(self.resonance_matrix[dim, other_dim])
            phase_shift += entanglement_strength * np.angle(self.quantum_states[other_dim, 0])

        # Convert the complex scalar to a numpy array for compatibility with C function
        state_np = np.array([state], dtype=np.complex128)

        # Call the C function
        result = self.c_lib.phase_rotation(state_np, phase_shift)

        if result != 0:
            raise RuntimeError(f"Error in phase_rotation for dimension {dim}, error code: {result}")

        # Return the transformed state as a complex scalar
        return state_np[0]

Python

import numpy as np
import ctypes

class MemoryBank:
    def __init__(self, capacity: int, dimensions: int):
        # ... (previous code)

        # Define argument types and return types for the C functions
        self.c_lib.initialize_bank.argtypes = [ctypes.POINTER(ctypes.c_void_p), ctypes.c_int, ctypes.c_int]
        self.c_lib.initialize_bank.restype = ctypes.c_int

        self.c_lib.store_data.argtypes = [ctypes.POINTER(ctypes.c_void_p), np.ctypeslib.ndpointer(np.complex128, flags="C_CONTIGUOUS")]
        self.c_lib.store_data.restype = ctypes.c_int

        self.c_lib.retrieve_data.argtypes = [ctypes.POINTER(ctypes.c_void_p), ctypes.c_int, np.ctypeslib.ndpointer(np.complex128, flags="C_CONTIGUOUS")]
        self.c_lib.retrieve_data.restype = ctypes.c_int

        self.c_lib.get_recent_data.argtypes = [ctypes.POINTER(ctypes.c_void_p), ctypes.c_int, np.ctypeslib.ndpointer(np.complex128, flags="C_CONTIGUOUS")]
        self.c_lib.get_recent_data.restype = ctypes.c_int

        self.c_lib.free_bank.argtypes = [ctypes.POINTER(ctypes.c_void_p)]
        self.c_lib.free_bank.restype = None

        # Initialize the bank using the C function
        self.bank_ptr = ctypes.c_void_p()
        result = self.c_lib.initialize_bank(ctypes.byref(self.bank_ptr), ctypes.c_int(self.capacity), ctypes.c_int(self.dimensions))
        if result != 0:
            raise RuntimeError(f"Error initializing memory bank, error code: {result}")

    def store(self, data_vector: np.ndarray):
        """Store a data vector in the memory bank."""
        # ...
        # Store data using the C function
        result = self.c_lib.store_data(self.bank_ptr, data_vector)
        if result != 0:
            raise RuntimeError(f"Error storing data in memory bank, error code: {result}")

    def retrieve(self, index: int) -> np.ndarray:
        """Retrieve a data vector from a specific index in the memory bank."""
        # ...
        # Retrieve data using the C function
        retrieved_vector = np.zeros(self.dimensions, dtype=np.complex128)
        result = self.c_lib.retrieve_data(self.bank_ptr, ctypes.c_int(index), retrieved_vector)
        if result != 0:
            raise RuntimeError(f"Error retrieving data from memory bank at index {index}, error code: {result}")
        return retrieved_vector

    def get_recent(self, n: int) -> np.ndarray:
        """Retrieve the n most recently stored data vectors."""
        # ...
        # Get recent data using the C function
        recent_data = np.zeros((n, self.dimensions), dtype=np.complex128)
        result = self.c_lib.get_recent_data(self.bank_ptr, ctypes.c_int(n), recent_data)
        if result != 0:
            raise RuntimeError(f"Error getting recent data from memory bank, error code: {result}")
        return recent_data

    # ...

Updating the C Backend
Implementing a Placeholder for FFTW-based FFT
In core_processing.c, add the following placeholder for the FFTW implementation:

c
Copy code
#include <stdio.h>
#include <stdlib.h>
#include <math.h>
#include <complex.h>
#include <fftw3.h>
#include "core_processing.h"

// Function to perform complex Fast Fourier Transform (FFT) using FFTW
void complex_fft_fftw(double complex *data, int n) {
fftw_complex *in, *out;
fftw_plan p;

in = (fftw_complex*) fftw_malloc(sizeof(fftw_complex) * n);
out = (fftw_complex*) fftw_malloc(sizeof(fftw_complex) * n);
p = fftw_plan_dft_1d(n, in, out, FFTW_FORWARD, FFTW_ESTIMATE);

for (int i = 0; i < n; i++) {
    in[i][0] = creal(data[i]);
    in[i][1] = cimag(data[i]);
}

fftw_execute(p);

for (int i = 0; i < n; i++) {
    data[i] = out[i][0] + I * out[i][1];
}

fftw_destroy_plan(p);
fftw_free(in);
fftw_free(out);

}

import numpy as np
import scipy.linalg as spl

import ctypes

class QuantumEngine:
def init(self, dimensions: int = 12, banks_per_dimension: int = 3):
# ... existing initialization code ...

    # Load the shared library for C backend
    self.c_lib = ctypes.CDLL("./c_backend/kaleidoscope_core.so")  # Adjust path if necessary

    # Define argument and return types for complex_fft_fftw
    self.c_lib.complex_fft_fftw.argtypes = [np.ctypeslib.ndpointer(np.complex128, flags="C_CONTIGUOUS"), ctypes.c_int]
    self.c_lib.complex_fft_fftw.restype = None

    # Define argument and return types for complex_array_add and complex_array_multiply
    self.c_lib.complex_array_add.argtypes = [
        np.ctypeslib.ndpointer(np.complex128, flags="C_CONTIGUOUS"),

        np.ctypeslib.ndpointer(np.complex128, flags="C_CONTIGUOUS"),
        ctypes.c_int

def _apply_quantum_transformations(self, data: np.ndarray) -> np.ndarray:
    transformed_data = np.zeros_like(data, dtype=np.complex128)

    for dim in range(self.dimensions):
        for bank in range(self.banks_per_dimension):
            # Existing quantum transformations
            hadamard_transformed = self._hadamard_transform(self.quantum_states[dim, bank])
            phase_transformed = self._apply_phase_rotation(hadamard_transformed, dim)
            self.quantum_states[dim, bank] = phase_transformed

            # Apply transformations to input data
            transformed_data[dim] += phase_transformed * data[dim, bank % data.shape[1]]

    # Apply FFT using the new FFTW-based C function
    for i in range(transformed_data.shape[0]):
        self.c_lib.complex_fft_fftw(transformed_data[i], transformed_data.shape[1])

    return transformed_data

# Add methods to use complex_array_add and complex_array_multiply
def add_complex_arrays(self, arr1: np.ndarray, arr2: np.ndarray) -> np.ndarray:
    """Add two complex arrays element-wise using the C backend."""
    if arr1.shape != arr2.shape:
        raise ValueError("Arrays must have the same shape for element-wise addition.")
    result = np.zeros_like(arr1)
    return_code = self.c_lib.complex_array_add(arr1, arr2, result, arr1.size)
    if return_code != 0:
        raise RuntimeError(f"Error in complex_array_add, error code: {return_code}")
    return result

Okay, let's implement a basic version of complex_fft_fftw using the FFTW library in your C backend.

1. Modify core_processing.c:
C

#include <stdio.h>
#include <stdlib.h>
#include <math.h>
#include <complex.h>
#include <fftw3.h>
#include "core_processing.h"

// ... (other functions)

// Function to perform complex Fast Fourier Transform (FFT) using FFTW
int complex_fft_fftw(double complex *data, int n) {
    fftw_complex *in, *out;
    fftw_plan p;

    // Allocate memory for FFTW input and output arrays
    in = (fftw_complex*) fftw_malloc(sizeof(fftw_complex) * n);
    if (in == NULL) {
        fprintf(stderr, "Memory allocation failed for FFTW input array\n");
        return 1; // Indicate memory allocation failure
    }

    out = (fftw_complex*) fftw_malloc(sizeof(fftw_complex) * n);
    if (out == NULL) {
        fprintf(stderr, "Memory allocation failed for FFTW output array\n");
        fftw_free(in);
        return 1; // Indicate memory allocation failure
    }

    // Create an FFTW plan for a 1D complex-to-complex transform
    p = fftw_plan_dft_1d(n, in, out, FFTW_FORWARD, FFTW_ESTIMATE);
    if (p == NULL) {
        fprintf(stderr, "Failed to create FFTW plan\n");
        fftw_free(in);
        fftw_free(out);
        return 2; // Indicate plan creation failure
    }

    // Copy data to FFTW input array and set the imaginary part to 0 if it's a real array
    for (int i = 0; i < n; i++) {
        in[i][0] = creal(data[i]); // Real part
        in[i][1] = cimag(data[i]); // Imaginary part
    }

    // Execute the FFTW plan
    fftw_execute(p);

    // Copy data from FFTW output array to the original data array
    for (int i = 0; i < n; i++) {
        data[i] = out[i][0] + I * out[i][1];
    }

    // Clean up FFTW resources
    fftw_destroy_plan(p);
    fftw_free(in);
    fftw_free(out);

    return 0; // Indicate success
}

// ... (rest of core_processing.c)

Explanation of Changes in core_processing.c:

    Include FFTW Header: We've included the fftw3.h header file to use FFTW functions.
    Memory Allocation: We're using fftw_malloc to allocate memory for the input and output arrays, as required by FFTW.
    Create Plan: fftw_plan_dft_1d creates an FFTW plan. We're using FFTW_FORWARD for a forward transform and FFTW_ESTIMATE for a reasonable balance between planning time and execution speed.
    Copy Data: We copy the data from the input data array to the FFTW input array in, correctly handling the real and imaginary parts.
    Execute Plan: fftw_execute performs the FFT.
    Copy Results Back: We copy the results from the FFTW output array out back to the original data array.
    Cleanup: fftw_destroy_plan destroys the plan, and fftw_free frees the allocated memory.
    Return Value: We return 0 to indicate success and non-zero values to indicate different types of errors.
